    19	- `quantbook-engine/crates/ql-calcgraph/src/topo.rs` (~462 lines)
    20	- `quantbook-engine/crates/ql-exec/src/calcgraph_session.rs` (lines 269-720 most relevant — `extract_and_register_deps`, `remove_formula_deps`, `on_set_formula`, `on_clear_formula`, `mark_dirty_from_cell_write`)
    21	
    22	## The three options the W5-47 handoff laid out
    23	
    24	**Option A — Delta-edge graph storage.** Add `Graph::clear_outgoing(node)` + `Graph::clear_range_deps_for_formula(node)` + `StripeIndex::clear_for_formula(node)`. Add reverse lookup `formula_to_stripe_keys: HashMap<NodeId, Vec<StripeKey>>` so the stripe clear is O(stripes-this-formula-is-in) not O(total-stripes). At extract_and_register_deps entry, call those clears before the new register pass. Estimated 3-5 days. Closes GAP-G-01 directly.
    25	
    26	**Option B — FormulaRegion binder + region detection.** At write-time, detect whether a formula matches a column-aligned region pattern and emit a `FormulaRegion` graph vertex that owns the range edge. Aligns with the Phase 4.7 array-formulas roadmap and with GAP-G-02 (SIMD region dispatch). Estimated 1-2 weeks. Closes G-02 + G-03 + most of G-01.
    27	
    28	**Option C — Lazy range expansion at recompute.** Keep Phase 0 graph contract. In `recompute_dirty`, before Tarjan, for each dirty formula F with range deps, walk dirty cells and inject temporary `F → cell` edges into the Tarjan input. Discard after. Estimated 2-3 days. Closes G-03 only; doesn't help G-01.
    29	
    30	W5-47 author recommendation: **Option A first** (correctness fix for G-01), then B or C for G-03 depending on Phase 4.7 bandwidth.
    31	
    32	## My (Claude's) preliminary view — for you to challenge
    33	
    34	The two bugs are **independent failure modes with independent fixes**, and conflating them into "pick one of A/B/C" is the wrong framing:
    35	
    36	- **GAP-G-01 fix → Option A only.** Add per-formula revocation API to Graph + StripeIndex; call it at extract_and_register_deps entry. The current `remove_formula_deps` at `calcgraph_session.rs:525` already cleans session-side reverse indices but explicitly does NOT touch graph edges / stripes ("Does NOT touch the Phase 0 `Graph`'s stripe state or edges (append-only contract)"). Extend it.
    37	
    38	- **GAP-G-03 fix → small Option C.** Add a Tarjan-input augmentation pass: for each dirty formula `F` with `formula_to_range_deps[F]`, for each dirty cell `c`, if `range_contains_rowcol(range, c)` then push a temp edge `F → c_node`. This lives inside `recompute_dirty` or as a new `topo::schedule_with_range_deps` variant. No graph storage change.
    39	
    40	- **Option B is the wrong tool for now.** FormulaRegion-as-binder is the Phase 4.7 design for array formulas + SIMD region dispatch (GAP-G-02). Region detection at write time is its own correctness surface (when does a new column-aligned formula belong to an existing region vs spawn a new one? what about non-uniform formulas that LOOK like a region but aren't?). Folding G-01/G-03 fixes into B couples them to a much bigger Phase 4.7 redesign that doesn't need to ship first.
    41	
    42	- **Combined A + C is the minimum surgical correctness fix.** Estimated 1 session for A (mostly mechanical: APIs + reverse lookup + wire into extract_and_register_deps + regression tests for the H3/H4 examples), 0.5-1 session for C (single Tarjan-input augmentation pass + regression tests for the H1 examples). Total ~1-1.5 sessions of implementation.
    43	
    44	- **Phase 4.3 V2 unblocks after both ship.** SUMIF/COUNTIF/lookup family all exercise range-as-edge correctness (GAP-G-03), so without C they'd ship onto a broken substrate. SUMIF over a rebound range would also exercise GAP-G-01.
    45	
    46	## What I want from you
    47	
    48	Independently — without anchoring to my view — answer:
    49	
    50	1. **Are G-01 and G-03 actually independent?** Or does one of the fixes accidentally regress the other? E.g., does Option C's temp-edge augmentation interact with Option A's removed stale stripes in some subtle way?
    51	
    52	2. **Is Option C correct?** Specifically: at recompute_dirty time, when we walk `formula_to_range_deps[F]` and inject temp edges to dirty cells in the range — are there cases where this produces a wrong cycle classification (e.g., a phantom cycle that wouldn't exist with proper edges)? The example A1 = SUM(B1:B1), B1 = A1+1: dirty set = {A1, B1}. Walk A1's range deps → B1 in range, B1 in dirty → temp edge A1→B1. Walk B1's deps → cell ref to A1 → real edge B1→A1. Tarjan sees A1→B1, B1→A1 → SCC of size 2 → cycled. Looks correct. But what about partial dirty sets? If only A1 is dirty and we're recomputing it because of a name change, B1 isn't dirty — the temp-edge pass adds nothing, Tarjan emits A1 alone, A1 evaluates against the CURRENT B1 value. Is that wrong? Excel canon: a name change should mark every dependent dirty. Phase 3.10 H2 fix made on_set_name BFS-fanout — so B1 would be dirty too via that fix. Reassure or refute this reasoning.
    53	
    54	3. **Is the Option A revocation API the smallest correct change to `Graph`?** Concretely: should `Graph::clear_outgoing(node)` also walk each former target's `incoming[target]` to remove the back-pointer? (Yes, for symmetry — but is the linear scan acceptable? Most cell formulas have ≤4 outgoing edges so the per-rebind cost is `Σ over edges of O(in_degree(target))`. For a heavy DAG with high in-degree nodes that could be costly — but how often does rebind hit such nodes?) What about determinism — `Vec` preserves insertion order, but after `clear_outgoing + re-extract` the new edges may be in a different order if the binder pass yields a different walk order; is that observable downstream? (My read: Tarjan's output ordering depends on insertion order via dfs_stack scan order — so yes, observable. But re-extract from the SAME plan should produce the SAME walk order, so as long as Plan→deps extraction is deterministic, the new edge order matches the old.)
    55	
    56	4. **Is there a simpler / better Option D I haven't considered?** For example:
    57	   - Mark edges with a per-formula epoch counter; sweep stale-epoch edges at scheduler time. Avoids removal API but adds epoch metadata to every edge.
    58	   - Switch `outgoing` to `Vec<IndexSet<NodeId>>` (preserves order AND allows O(1) remove). Adds a dep but simpler removal contract.
    59	   - Compute the scheduler-relevant edge set from scratch at every recompute_dirty by re-reading bound plans from the session — avoids stale-edge problem entirely but pays per-recompute extraction cost.
    60	
    61	5. **The W5-47 author claims Option A is a "3-5 day refactor" and re-test of Phase 3.1-3.8.** Is that accurate? Or is it actually smaller (the existing extract path already calls `remove_formula_deps`; we just expand its scope) — or larger (some test or invariant deeper in Phase 3 silently depends on the append-only contract)?
    62	
    63	6. **Stop conditions: what would invalidate the "ship A + C now, defer B to Phase 4.7" decision?**
    64	   - If Phase 4.3 V2 SUMIF/COUNTIF/lookup ALSO needs the FormulaRegion binder for non-correctness reasons.
    65	   - If Option A causes a subtle perf regression on the OG-02 25M-cell hot path.
    66	   - If the reverse `formula_to_stripe_keys` index materially inflates memory at scale.
    67	
    68	Be specific. Read the actual code. If you disagree, say so plainly and propose the alternative concretely. If you agree, say what you'd watch for during implementation.
    69	
    70	Treat this as an architectural review for a one-pager I'm writing into `docs/architecture/`. Your output should help me write that one-pager — verdict + reasoning + concrete implementation notes + risks.

Aligns with the Phase 4.7 array-formulas roadmap and with GAP-G-02 (SIMD region dispatch). Estimated 1-2 weeks. Closes G-02 + G-03 + most of G-01.
    40	
    41	**Option C — Lazy range expansion at recompute.** Keep Phase 0 graph contract. In `recompute_dirty`, before Tarjan, for each dirty formula F with range deps, walk dirty cells and inject temporary `F → cell` edges into the Tarjan input. Discard after. Estimated 2-3 days. Closes G-03 only; doesn't help G-01.
    42	
    43	W5-47 author recommendation: **Option A first** (correctness fix for G-01), then B or C for G-03 depending on Phase 4.7 bandwidth.
    44	
    45	## My (Claude's) preliminary view — for you to challenge
    46	
    47	The two bugs are **independent failure modes with independent fixes**, and conflating them into "pick one of A/B/C" is the wrong framing:
    48	
    49	- **GAP-G-01 fix → Option A only.** Add per-formula revocation API to Graph + StripeIndex; call it at extract_and_register_deps entry. The current `remove_formula_deps` at `calcgraph_session.rs:525` already cleans session-side reverse indices but explicitly does NOT touch graph edges / stripes ("Does NOT touch the Phase 0 `Graph`'s stripe state or edges (append-only contract)"). Extend it.
    50	
    51	- **GAP-G-03 fix → small Option C.** Add a Tarjan-input augmentation pass: for each dirty formula `F` with `formula_to_range_deps[F]`, for each dirty cell `c`, if `range_contains_rowcol(range, c)` then push a temp edge `F → c_node`. This lives inside `recompute_dirty` or as a new `topo::schedule_with_range_deps` variant. No graph storage change.
    52	
    53	- **Option B is the wrong tool for now.** FormulaRegion-as-binder is the Phase 4.7 design for array formulas + SIMD region dispatch (GAP-G-02). Region detection at write time is its own correctness surface (when does a new column-aligned formula belong to an existing region vs spawn a new one? what about non-uniform formulas that LOOK like a region but aren't?). Folding G-01/G-03 fixes into B couples them to a much bigger Phase 4.7 redesign that doesn't need to ship first.
    54	
    55	- **Combined A + C is the minimum surgical correctness fix.** Estimated 1 session for A (mostly mechanical: APIs + reverse lookup + wire into extract_and_register_deps + regression tests for the H3/H4 examples), 0.5-1 session for C (single Tarjan-input augmentation pass + regression tests for the H1 examples). Total ~1-1.5 sessions of implementation.
    56	
    57	- **Phase 4.3 V2 unblocks after both ship.** SUMIF/COUNTIF/lookup family all exercise range-as-edge correctness (GAP-G-03), so without C they'd ship onto a broken substrate. SUMIF over a rebound range would also exercise GAP-G-01.
    58	
    59	## What I want from you
    60	
    61	Independently — without anchoring to my view — answer:
    62	
    63	1. **Are G-01 and G-03 actually independent?** Or does one of the fixes accidentally regress the other? E.g., does Option C's temp-edge augmentation interact with Option A's removed stale stripes in some subtle way?
    64	
    65	2. **Is Option C correct?** Specifically: at recompute_dirty time, when we walk `formula_to_range_deps[F]` and inject temp edges to dirty cells in the range — are there cases where this produces a wrong cycle classification (e.g., a phantom cycle that wouldn't exist with proper edges)? The example A1 = SUM(B1:B1), B1 = A1+1: dirty set = {A1, B1}. Walk A1's range deps → B1 in range, B1 in dirty → temp edge A1→B1. Walk B1's deps → cell ref to A1 → real edge B1→A1. Tarjan sees A1→B1, B1→A1 → SCC of size 2 → cycled. Looks correct. But what about partial dirty sets? If only A1 is dirty and we're recomputing it because of a name change, B1 isn't dirty — the temp-edge pass adds nothing, Tarjan emits A1 alone, A1 evaluates against the CURRENT B1 value. Is that wrong? Excel canon: a name change should mark every dependent dirty. Phase 3.10 H2 fix made on_set_name BFS-fanout — so B1 would be dirty too via that fix. Reassure or refute this reasoning.
    66	
    67	3. **Is the Option A revocation API the smallest correct change to `Graph`?** Concretely: should `Graph::clear_outgoing(node)` also walk each former target's `incoming[target]` to remove the back-pointer? (Yes, for symmetry — but is the linear scan acceptable? Most cell formulas have ≤4 outgoing edges so the per-rebind cost is `Σ over edges of O(in_degree(target))`. For a heavy DAG with high in-degree nodes that could be costly — but how often does rebind hit such nodes?) What about determinism — `Vec` preserves insertion order, but after `clear_outgoing + re-extract` the new edges may be in a different order if the binder pass yields a different walk order; is that observable downstream? (My read: Tarjan's output ordering depends on insertion order via dfs_stack scan order — so yes, observable. But re-extract from the SAME plan should produce the SAME walk order, so as long as Plan→deps extraction is deterministic, the new edge order matches the old.)
    68	
    69	4. **Is there a simpler / better Option D I haven't considered?** For example:
    70	   - Mark edges with a per-formula epoch counter; sweep stale-epoch edges at scheduler time. Avoids removal API but adds epoch metadata to every edge.
    71	   - Switch `outgoing` to `Vec<IndexSet<NodeId>>` (preserves order AND allows O(1) remove). Adds a dep but simpler removal contract.
    72	   - Compute the scheduler-relevant edge set from scratch at every recompute_dirty by re-reading bound plans from the session — avoids stale-edge problem entirely but pays per-recompute extraction cost.
    73	
    74	5. **The W5-47 author claims Option A is a "3-5 day refactor" and re-test of Phase 3.1-3.8.** Is that accurate? Or is it actually smaller (the existing extract path already calls `remove_formula_deps`; we just expand its scope) — or larger (some test or invariant deeper in Phase 3 silently depends on the append-only contract)?
    75	
    76	6. **Stop conditions: what would invalidate the "ship A + C now, defer B to Phase 4.7" decision?**
    77	   - If Phase 4.3 V2 SUMIF/COUNTIF/lookup ALSO needs the FormulaRegion binder for non-correctness reasons.
    78	   - If Option A causes a subtle perf regression on the OG-02 25M-cell hot path.
    79	   - If the reverse `formula_to_stripe_keys` index materially inflates memory at scale.
    80	
    81	Be specific. Read the actual code. If you disagree, say so plainly and propose the alternative concretely. If you agree, say what you'd watch for during implementation.
    82

    18	
    19	### W5-49 artifacts (architectural decision)
    20	- `quantbook-engine/docs/architecture/2026-05-13-graph-storage-decision.md` — the one-pager. Validate against the actual implementation in W5-50.

    22	
    23	### W5-50 — graph correctness fix (THE MAIN AUDIT TARGET)
    24	- `quantbook-engine/crates/ql-calcgraph/src/stripes.rs` — read entirely. Focus on:
    25	  - New `formula_to_stripe_keys` reverse index field.
    26	  - `insert` function — does it correctly track the reverse mirror when the forward HashSet already contains the formula?
    27	  - `clear_for_formula` — does it correctly prune empty stripes? Idempotent? Handles unseen NodeId?
    28	  - `pub fn range_contains_rowcol` — promoted from `pub(crate)`. Are all callers safe with public visibility?
    29	- `quantbook-engine/crates/ql-calcgraph/src/edges.rs` — read entirely. Focus on:
    30	  - `AdjacencyVectors::clear_outgoing` — drains outgoing[from] but only returns drained list; does NOT touch incoming. Caller (Graph::clear_outgoing) walks targets to clear back-pointers. Symmetric invariant?
    31	  - `remove_back_pointer` — `retain` removes ALL matching back-pointers. Is "all" the right semantics? What if `add_edge(F, T)` was called twice (Phase 0 allows duplicates) — `clear_outgoing` would drain BOTH outgoing entries, then `remove_back_pointer` would remove BOTH incoming entries. Consistent? Or could there be a case where one was added by a DIFFERENT formula and gets wrongly removed?
    32	- `quantbook-engine/crates/ql-calcgraph/src/graph.rs` — read entirely. Focus on:
    33	  - `Graph::clear_outgoing(node)` — calls edges.clear_outgoing then walks targets to remove_back_pointer. Bumps revision once. Idempotent claim?
    34	  - `Graph::clear_range_deps_for_formula(node)` — clears formula_to_range_deps + calls stripes.clear_for_formula. Bumps revision once. Idempotent?
    35	  - `Graph::range_deps_for(node)` — returns &[RangeRef] or empty slice. Used by session-side supplemental builder.
    36	  - `Graph::range_ref_sheet(range)` — static helper. Correct for all three RangeRef variants (Cells, WholeColumn, WholeRow)?
    37	- `quantbook-engine/crates/ql-calcgraph/src/topo.rs` — read entirely. Focus on:
    38	  - `schedule_with_supplemental` — new public entry point. Now also called by the wrapper `schedule(graph, dirty_subset)`. The new signature accepts a `&HashMap<NodeId, Vec<NodeId>>`.
    39	  - `tarjan_dfs` modified to iterate `outgoing + supplemental` via index split (children index < outgoing.len() uses real graph; >= uses supplemental). Off-by-one? Indexing safety?
    40	  - Self-loop classification at SCC pop: now checks BOTH `graph.outgoing(scc[0])` AND `supplemental.get(&scc[0])`. Is the OR correct? Should it be AND? Either present?
    41	  - Determinism: the supplemental map's iteration order is HashMap-randomized, BUT we don't iterate the map — we look up by key. So order of supplemental_for_v depends on the Vec inside, which is built in `build_range_supplemental` from a sorted iteration. Check end-to-end determinism.
    42	- `quantbook-engine/crates/ql-exec/src/calcgraph_session.rs` — read lines 410-720 (the rebind path + supplemental builder + schedule_dirty). Focus on:
    43	  - `extract_and_register_deps` now calls `self.graph.clear_outgoing(formula_node)` and `self.graph.clear_range_deps_for_formula(formula_node)` AFTER `remove_formula_deps`. Order matters? Is `remove_formula_deps` redundant now or still needed for session-side reverse indices?
    44	  - `build_range_supplemental` — for each dirty formula F with range deps, scan all dirty formulas G. The inner check: `gs != resolved_sheet` skips cross-sheet; `range_contains_rowcol(range, gr, gc)` checks bounds. Does NOT exclude `g == f` — self-range cycles intentional. Is the self-edge for a formula whose range covers itself but isn't really cyclic (e.g., A1 = SUM(A1:A10) where A1 is the FIRST cell — Excel treats this as a cycle) handled correctly?
    45	  - Determinism of the supplemental map: dirty_vec is sorted by NodeId. dirty_addrs is built from dirty_vec in order. The inner `for &(g, ...) in &dirty_addrs` iterates in sorted order. So `supplemental[F]` ends up in sorted-by-G order. Confirm.
    46	  - **A potential subtle bug**: `schedule_dirty` drains `self.dirty` into `dirty_vec`. THEN calls `build_range_supplemental(&dirty_vec)`. But by this point, `self.dirty` is EMPTY. `build_range_supplemental` looks at `cell_address_for(n)` which uses `self.cell_index` — that's fine. But it does NOT actually look at `self.dirty`. So that's OK. Verify.
    47	  - Does the supplemental include ALL dirty formula nodes including non-cell-address nodes (e.g., a hypothetical RangeNode or FormulaRegionNode in the dirty set)? The `cell_address_for` filter handles that — non-Cell nodes are filtered out. But should the supplemental be considered there at all? FormulaRegion nodes might be in the dirty set in Phase 4.7. For now, filtering out is correct.
    48

    51	- `quantbook-engine/crates/ql-calcgraph/src/edges.rs::tests` — 4 new tests.

    56	### W5-51 — trig batch
    57	- `quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/scalar_fns.rs` lines 715-855 (the new SIN/COS/TAN/ASIN/ACOS/ATAN/ATAN2 definitions). Focus on:
    58	  - TAN(π/2): claim is "huge-finite, not error". IEEE 754 says (π/2).tan() is finite because π/2 isn't exactly representable. Verify with actual `f64::consts::FRAC_PI_2.tan()` value. Test `tan_near_half_pi_is_huge_finite_not_error` asserts `v > 1e10` — is that tight? Actual value is ≈1.6e16.
    59	  - ASIN/ACOS domain check uses `if !(-1.0..=1.0).contains(&n)`. Does this correctly reject NaN? `RangeInclusive::contains` for NaN returns false (NaN is not <= or >= anything), so `!contains(NaN)` is true → returns #NUM!. But Rust's `f64::asin(NaN)` returns NaN, which `sanitize_f64` would also surface as #NUM!. Either way OK, but the redundancy means the explicit check is somewhat defensive.
    60	  - ATAN2 arg-order claim: comment says "Excel arg order is `(x, y)` historically — but the W3C / OOXML standard and modern Excel use `ATAN2(x_num, y_num)`." Then the code does `y.atan2(x)` where `x = args[0]`, `y = args[1]`. So `ATAN2(args[0]=x, args[1]=y)` → `y.atan2(x)`. **Verify this matches Excel canon.** Excel docs: `ATAN2(x_num, y_num)` returns arctangent of y_num/x_num. Rust's `f64::atan2(y, x)` returns arctangent of y/x. So `args[1].atan2(args[0])` = y_num.atan2(x_num) = arctan(y/x). ✓
    61	    - Test `atan2_basic_quadrants`: ATAN2(1, 0) → 0 (along +x). atan2(0_y, 1_x) = arctan(0/1) = 0. ✓
    62	    - ATAN2(0, 1) → π/2 (along +y). atan2(1_y, 0_x) = arctan(1/0) = π/2. ✓
    63	    - ATAN2(-1, 0) → π (along -x). atan2(0_y, -1_x) = arctan(0/-1) → π (since x<0, y=+0). ✓
    64	    - ATAN2(1, 1) → π/4. atan2(1, 1) = π/4. ✓
    65	  - ATAN2(0, 0) → #DIV/0!. IronCalc: confirm this matches.
    66	- `quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/registry.rs` — 7 new entries; expected-count test updated to 59.
    67	- `quantbook-engine/docs/compat/excel-matrix.md` — trig row flipped to ✅, Tests=13. Does the script's coverage stay at 40%?
    68

    86	## Specific concerns to evaluate
    87	
    88	### CONCERN-1: Function-signature scope discovery (Phase 4.3 V2)
    89	
    90	After W5-51 shipped, the operator surfaced a hidden scope issue: the function signature `ScalarFn = fn(&[Value]) -> Value` does NOT support range-aware functions (SUMIF/COUNTIF/VLOOKUP). The W5-49 decision doc did not mention this. Is this a:
    91	- (a) Legitimate scope discovery — W5-49 was about graph correctness, not function-call infrastructure.
    92	- (b) Doc gap — the implementation outline should have called out that SUMIF would need additional infra beyond just Option C.
    93	
    94	Codex's pre-decision review listed stop conditions including "Phase 4.3 V2 actually requires FormulaRegion for SEMANTICS (not just perf)". Did Codex implicitly assume the function infra would just work? If so, that's a Codex miss too.
    95	
    96	### CONCERN-2: Determinism of build_range_supplemental
    97	
    98	`schedule_dirty` drains `self.dirty: HashSet<NodeId>` into `dirty_vec` then sorts. Good. But `dirty_addrs` is built from `dirty_vec.iter().filter_map(...)`. Iteration order preserved. The inner `for &(g, ...) in &dirty_addrs` iterates in the same order. So `supplemental[F]` ends up with G's in sorted-by-NodeId order.
    99	
   100	BUT: the OUTER `for &(f, fs, _, _) in &dirty_addrs` is also in sorted order. So the HashMap's insertion order is sorted-by-F-NodeId. Determinism downstream of HashMap depends on iteration order (HashMap is randomized). The CONSUMER of `supplemental` is `tarjan_dfs`, which looks up by key (`supplemental.get(&v)`) — so the randomized iteration order doesn't matter. Only the per-entry Vec order matters, and that IS sorted. ✓
   101	
   102	But there's a subtle point: in Tarjan, the order of children visited at each frame affects the resulting SCC discovery order. For a deterministic schedule output, the per-frame child order must be deterministic. Real outgoing edges come from `Vec<NodeId>` (insertion order — deterministic). Supplemental edges come from `supplemental[v]` which IS sorted. So combined order = (real edges in insertion order) ++ (supplemental in sorted order). Deterministic.
   103	
   104	UNLESS: real edges themselves are NOT in deterministic insertion order across runs (e.g., the extract_and_register_deps walk order depends on a HashMap iteration). The walk_plan_for_deps walker is recursive over ExprPlan AST — fully deterministic. Then `deduplicate` uses `HashSet` + `retain` — retain preserves order. So insertion order is the AST walk order. Deterministic. ✓
   105	
   106	But hold on — re-bind: `clear_outgoing` drains and `extract_and_register_deps` re-adds. So if a formula's plan is identical, the new outgoing list should equal the old. Determinism test (`determinism_rebind_same_plan_same_edges` in graph.rs::tests) verifies this. ✓
   107	
   108	OK determinism looks correct. **But please double-check this independently — I might be missing a subtle case.**
   109	
   110	### CONCERN-3: Self-edge in supplemental
   111	
   112	`build_range_supplemental` does NOT exclude `g == f`. This is intentional for `A1 = SUM(A1:A1)` self-range cycles. Test `self_range_cycle_via_supplemental` confirms this works.
   113	
   114	But what about `A1 = SUM(A1:A10)` where A1 is the cell that contains the SUM formula and A1 is inside the SUM range? Excel: this is a circular reference. My implementation: supplemental[A1] = [A1] (since A1 is in A1:A10). Tarjan emits A1 as cycled. ✓
   115	
   116	What about a case where the range covers F but F shouldn't be self-referencing? E.g., F is at C1 with `=SUM(A1:Z1)` — F is NOT in the range (column C is in A1:Z1 columns). The range covers (row 1, cols A..Z). F is at (row 1, col C) → IS in the range. supplemental[F] = [F] if F is dirty. Tarjan emits F as cycled. **WAIT** — is that wrong? Excel would treat `=SUM(A1:Z1)` at C1 as a self-cycle because C1 is in A1:Z1. ✓ this is correct.
   117	
   118	OK self-edge handling looks correct.
   119	
   120	### CONCERN-4: Cross-sheet range deps
   121	
   122	`build_range_supplemental` resolves the range's sheet to `range_ref_sheet(range).unwrap_or(fs)` (F's owning sheet). Then checks `gs == resolved_sheet`. For ranges with `sheet: Some(other_sheet)`, this correctly skips G's on F's sheet.
   123	
   124	BUT: today the binder always sets `sheet: Some(_)` per Phase 3.3 comment. Cross-sheet refs aren't fully wired (Phase 4.6). So in practice this matters mostly for the (likely) future when cross-sheet ranges become real. Until then, all range deps are intra-sheet. **Is this still correct for the future cross-sheet case?**
   125	
   126	### CONCERN-5: W5-51 trig — what's NOT covered
   127	
   128	- SINH / COSH / TANH (hyperbolic) — deferred to wave 2 per matrix. Fine.
   129	- ASINH / ACOSH / ATANH — same. Fine.
   130	- Are there any trig edge cases the tests miss?
   131	  - Negative zero: TAN(-0.0) → should be -0.0 or 0.0? Test doesn't cover.
   132	  - Subnormals: SIN(f64::MIN_POSITIVE) → ~0. Test doesn't cover.
   133	  - Infinity input: SIN(f64::INFINITY) → NaN → sanitize_f64 → #NUM!. Test doesn't cover.
   134	- Coverage script still at 40% — is this honest? The 7 trig functions are one row in the matrix; the matrix has 217 rows total. Adding 7 functions in one row doesn't move the row count, but it does change the count of ✅ rows. Was the matrix-coverage-reporter script run after the matrix update? Verify.
   135	
   136	### CONCERN-6: Existing test failures in light of new code
   137	
   138	W5-50 adds `clear_outgoing` to extract_and_register_deps. This means EVERY on_set_formula (including re-binds to the SAME plan) now does a clear-then-reinsert. Performance: for a single-formula rebind this is negligible. For a workbook with 1M formulas being initially loaded via rebuild_from_workbook (which calls extract_and_register_deps per formula), each formula now does an O(1) reverse-index drop on a HashMap entry that doesn't exist yet (idempotent no-op). Should be fast.
   139	
   140	But: does the FIRST-EVER bind of a formula trigger a no-op clear path that's actually cheap? Verify by reading the code:
   141	- `clear_outgoing(formula_node)` calls `edges.clear_outgoing(formula_node)` which does `std::mem::take(&mut self.outgoing[from.index()])` — drains a `Vec<NodeId>` that's empty → returns empty vec. Then walks the empty drained list → no back-pointer removal. Bumps revision once.
   142	- `clear_range_deps_for_formula(formula_node)` removes from `formula_to_range_deps` (a HashMap remove — fast even if absent). Then calls `stripes.clear_for_formula(formula_node)` which does `formula_to_stripe_keys.remove(&formula_node)` — fast remove of absent key.
   143	
   144	OK fast. No perf concern.
   145	
   146	### CONCERN-7: Cargo audit — was it re-run?
   147	
   148	The commit messages claim "cargo audit ✓ (1 pre-existing allowed warning)". Verify that the actual current `cargo audit` output matches. The previous run (in W5-50) said this; W5-51 should not have changed dependencies. But verify.

    42	- `quantbook-engine/crates/ql-calcgraph/src/edges.rs` — read entirely. Focus on:

    44	  - `remove_back_pointer` — `retain` removes ALL matching back-pointers. Is "all" the right semantics? What if `add_edge(F, T)` was called twice (Phase 0 allows duplicates) — `clear_outgoing` would drain BOTH outgoing entries, then `remove_back_pointer` would remove BOTH incoming entries. Consistent? Or could there be a case where one was added by a DIFFERENT formula and gets wrongly removed?
    45	- `quantbook-engine/crates/ql-calcgraph/src/graph.rs` — read entirely. Focus on:

    47	  - `Graph::clear_range_deps_for_formula(node)` — clears formula_to_range_deps + calls stripes.clear_for_formula. Bumps revision once. Idempotent?

    51	  - `schedule_with_supplemental` — new public entry point. Now also called by the wrapper `schedule(graph, dirty_subset)`. The new signature accepts a `&HashMap<NodeId, Vec<NodeId>>`.
    52	  - `tarjan_dfs` modified to iterate `outgoing + supplemental` via index split (children index < outgoing.len() uses real graph; >= uses supplemental). Off-by-one? Indexing safety?
    53	  - Self-loop classification at SCC pop: now checks BOTH `graph.outgoing(scc[0])` AND `supplemental.get(&scc[0])`. Is the OR correct? Should it be AND? Either present?
    54	  - Determinism: the supplemental map's iteration order is HashMap-randomized, BUT we don't iterate the map — we look up by key. So order of supplemental_for_v depends on the Vec inside, which is built in `build_range_supplemental` from a sorted iteration. Check end-to-end determinism.
    55	- `quantbook-engine/crates/ql-exec/src/calcgraph_session.rs` — read lines 410-720 (the rebind path + supplemental builder + schedule_dirty). Focus on:
    56	  - `extract_and_register_deps` now calls `self.graph.clear_outgoing(formula_node)` and `self.graph.clear_range_deps_for_formula(formula_node)` AFTER `remove_formula_deps`. Order matters? Is `remove_formula_deps` redundant now or still needed for session-side reverse indices?
    57	  - `build_range_supplemental` — for each dirty formula F with range deps, scan all dirty formulas G. The inner check: `gs != resolved_sheet` skips cross-sheet; `range_contains_rowcol(range, gr, gc)` checks bounds. Does NOT exclude `g == f` — self-range cycles intentional. Is the self-edge for a formula whose range covers itself but isn't really cyclic (e.g., A1 = SUM(A1:A10) where A1 is the FIRST cell — Excel treats this as a cycle) handled correctly?
    58	  - Determinism of the supplemental map: dirty_vec is sorted by NodeId. dirty_addrs is built from dirty_vec in order. The inner `for &(g, ...) in &dirty_addrs` iterates in sorted order. So `supplemental[F]` ends up in sorted-by-G order. Confirm.
    59	  - **A potential subtle bug**: `schedule_dirty` drains `self.dirty` into `dirty_vec`. THEN calls `build_range_supplemental(&dirty_vec)`. But by this point, `self.dirty` is EMPTY. `build_range_supplemental` looks at `cell_address_for(n)` which uses `self.cell_index` — that's fine. But it does NOT actually look at `self.dirty`. So that's OK. Verify.
    60	  - Does the supplemental include ALL dirty formula nodes including non-cell-address nodes (e.g., a hypothetical RangeNode or FormulaRegionNode in the dirty set)? The `cell_address_for` filter handles that — non-Cell nodes are filtered out. But should the supplemental be considered there at all? FormulaRegion nodes might be in the dirty set in Phase 4.7. For now, filtering out is correct.

   116	
   117	UNLESS: real edges themselves are NOT in deterministic insertion order across runs (e.g., the extract_and_register_deps walk order depends on a HashMap iteration). The walk_plan_for_deps walker is recursive over ExprPlan AST — fully deterministic. Then `deduplicate` uses `HashSet` + `retain` — retain preserves order. So insertion order is the AST walk order. Deterministic. ✓
   118	
   119	But hold on — re-bind: `clear_outgoing` drains and `extract_and_register_deps` re-adds. So if a formula's plan is identical, the new outgoing list should equal the old. Determinism test (`determinism_rebind_same_plan_same_edges` in graph.rs::tests) verifies this. ✓
   120	
   121	OK determinism looks correct. **But please double-check this independently — I might be missing a subtle case.**
   122	
   123	### CONCERN-3: Self-edge in supplemental
   124	
   125	`build_range_supplemental` does NOT exclude `g == f`. This is intentional for `A1 = SUM(A1:A1)` self-range cycles. Test `self_range_cycle_via_supplemental` confirms this works.
   126	
   127	But what about `A1 = SUM(A1:A10)` where A1 is the cell that contains the SUM formula and A1 is inside the SUM range? Excel: this is a circular reference. My implementation: supplemental[A1] = [A1] (since A1 is in A1:A10). Tarjan emits A1 as cycled. ✓
   128	
   129	What about a case where the range covers F but F shouldn't be self-referencing? E.g., F is at C1 with `=SUM(A1:Z1)` — F is NOT in the range (column C is in A1:Z1 columns). The range covers (row 1, cols A..Z). F is at (row 1, col C) → IS in the range. supplemental[F] = [F] if F is dirty. Tarjan emits F as cycled. **WAIT** — is that wrong? Excel would treat `=SUM(A1:Z1)` at C1 as a self-cycle because C1 is in A1:Z1. ✓ this is correct.
   130	
   131	OK self-edge handling looks correct.
   132	
   133	### CONCERN-4: Cross-sheet range deps
   134	
   135	`build_range_supplemental` resolves the range's sheet to `range_ref_sheet(range).unwrap_or(fs)` (F's owning sheet). Then checks `gs == resolved_sheet`. For ranges with `sheet: Some(other_sheet)`, this correctly skips G's on F's sheet.
   136	
   137	BUT: today the binder always sets `sheet: Some(_)` per Phase 3.3 comment. Cross-sheet refs aren't fully wired (Phase 4.6). So in practice this matters mostly for the (likely) future when cross-sheet ranges become real. Until then, all range deps are intra-sheet. **Is this still correct for the future cross-sheet case?**
   138	
   139	### CONCERN-5: W5-51 trig — what's NOT covered
   140	
   141	- SINH / COSH / TANH (hyperbolic) — deferred to wave 2 per matrix. Fine.
   142	- ASINH / ACOSH / ATANH — same. Fine.
   143	- Are there any trig edge cases the tests miss?
   144	  - Negative zero: TAN(-0.0) → should be -0.0 or 0.0? Test doesn't cover.
   145	  - Subnormals: SIN(f64::MIN_POSITIVE) → ~0. Test doesn't cover.
   146	  - Infinity input: SIN(f64::INFINITY) → NaN → sanitize_f64 → #NUM!. Test doesn't cover.
   147	- Coverage script still at 40% — is this honest? The 7 trig functions are one row in the matrix; the matrix has 217 rows total. Adding 7 functions in one row doesn't move the row count, but it does change the count of ✅ rows. Was the matrix-coverage-reporter script run after the matrix update? Verify.
   148	
   149	### CONCERN-6: Existing test failures in light of new code
   150	
   151	W5-50 adds `clear_outgoing` to extract_and_register_deps. This means EVERY on_set_formula (including re-binds to the SAME plan) now does a clear-then-reinsert. Performance: for a single-formula rebind this is negligible. For a workbook with 1M formulas being initially loaded via rebuild_from_workbook (which calls extract_and_register_deps per formula), each formula now does an O(1) reverse-index drop on a HashMap entry that doesn't exist yet (idempotent no-op). Should be fast.
   152	
   153	But: does the FIRST-EVER bind of a formula trigger a no-op clear path that's actually cheap? Verify by reading the code:
   154	- `clear_outgoing(formula_node)` calls `edges.clear_outgoing(formula_node)` which does `std::mem::take(&mut self.outgoing[from.index()])` — drains a `Vec<NodeId>` that's empty → returns empty vec. Then walks the empty drained list → no back-pointer removal. Bumps revision once.
   155	- `clear_range_deps_for_formula(formula_node)` removes from `formula_to_range_deps` (a HashMap remove — fast even if absent). Then calls `stripes.clear_for_formula(formula_node)` which does `formula_to_stripe_keys.remove(&formula_node)` — fast remove of absent key.
   156	
   157	OK fast. No perf concern.

     1	# Sonnet mega-audit — W5-49/50/51
     2	
     3	## Verdict (one paragraph)
     4	
     5	W5-50 is the most important commit and its core correctness logic is sound: the Graph revocation APIs (`clear_outgoing`, `clear_range_deps_for_formula`), the StripeIndex reverse index, and the Tarjan supplemental adjacency all implement the W5-49 decision faithfully. The H3/H4/H1 scenario tests are genuine and pass. However, there is one confirmed HIGH bug: `on_clear_formula` calls `remove_formula_deps` but NOT `graph.clear_outgoing` or `graph.clear_range_deps_for_formula`, leaving stale graph edges and stripe entries behind whenever a formula cell is cleared (rather than rebound). This is the same class of staleness that W5-50 was specifically built to fix — and it was missed for the clear path. W5-51 (trig batch) is correct but has a doc-title error in the `atan2` rustdoc (says `ATAN2(y, x)` in the signature line but the implementation and tests use the correct `(x, y)` Excel order). W5-49 is a decision doc and its claims about Codex effort estimates are honest. No false cycles, no determinism breaks, and no test-count fraud were found, though per-file counts in commit messages have minor off-by-one errors that net to zero.

The actual W5-50 implementation wires into `schedule_dirty`, which is called by `recompute_dirty` — one level of indirection removed. This is fully correct but the phrasing "Wire into `recompute_dirty`" is inaccurate at the literal call-site level. Low honesty impact; the behavior is right.

    26	## W5-50 (graph correctness fix — the main audit target)
    27	
    28	### HIGH
    29	
    30	**BUG: `on_clear_formula` does not revoke graph edges or stripe entries.**
    31	
    32	File: `quantbook-engine/crates/ql-exec/src/calcgraph_session.rs`, lines 721-731.
    33	
    34	```rust
    35	pub fn on_clear_formula(&mut self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId) {
    36	    self.hook_counts.clear_formula = self.hook_counts.clear_formula.saturating_add(1);
    37	    if let Some(node) = self.cell_index.get(&(sheet, row, col)).copied() {
    38	        self.remove_formula_deps(node);   // ← session-side only
    39	        self.dirty.remove(&node);
    40	    }
    41	    self.mark_dirty_from_cell_write(sheet, row, col);
    42	}
    43	```
    44	
    45	`remove_formula_deps`'s own docstring at line 533 says: "Does NOT touch the `Graph`'s stripe state or edges — those are revoked separately by the caller via `Graph::clear_outgoing` + `Graph::clear_range_deps_for_formula` (W5-50)." The caller here is `on_clear_formula`, and it does neither. The result: clearing a formula that previously depended on A1 leaves a stale outgoing edge from the cleared formula's NodeId pointing to A1, and any range deps remain in the stripe index. Concrete failure:
    46	
    47	```
    48	1. set_formula(B1, "=A1")   → outgoing[B1] = [A1], incoming[A1] = [B1]
    49	2. set_formula(A1, "=B1")   → outgoing[A1] = [B1], Tarjan: A1↔B1 cycle → #CIRC!
    50	3. clear_formula(B1)        → session-side: remove_formula_deps(B1), dirty.remove(B1)
    51	                            → BUT outgoing[B1] still = [A1], incoming[A1] still = [B1]
    52	4. (later edit that makes both A1 and B1 dirty)
    53	   schedule_dirty()         → Tarjan sees stale B1→A1 + real A1→B1 → false #CIRC! again
    54	```
    55	
    56	This is the exact H3 class of failure that W5-50 was designed to eliminate, reproduced on the clear path. The fix requires adding two lines inside the `if let Some(node)` block:
    57	
    58	```rust
    59	self.graph.clear_outgoing(node);
    60	self.graph.clear_range_deps_for_formula(node);
    61	```
    62	
    63	No test covers the clear path for graph-level staleness — the existing test `clear_formula_evicts_self_and_dirties_downstream` (line 1883) only checks the session-side dirty set, not graph edges or stripe state.
    64	
    65	### MEDIUM
    66	
    67	**build_range_supplemental produces duplicate G entries when F has multiple ranges that both contain G.**
    68	
    69	File: `calcgraph_session.rs`, lines 1002-1012.
    70	
    71	The inner loop over `ranges` iterates per range; for each range it iterates the full `dirty_addrs` list. If F has `SUM(A:A) + SUM(A:A)` (same range twice — possible after a re-bind if the dedup in `extract_and_register_deps` missed it) or more practically `SUM(A1:A10)` and `SUM(A1:A5)` (two ranges that both contain the same G at A3), G will be pushed twice into `supplemental[F]`. Tarjan re-traverses the back-edge on the second hit but handles it correctly (the `indices.contains_key` check prevents re-visiting). However, the Vec grows unnecessarily, and the topo test `supplemental_does_not_duplicate_existing_real_edge` only covers real-edge + supplemental-edge deduplication, not supplemental + supplemental. This is a latent inefficiency but not a correctness bug today. It will worsen as Phase 4.3 V2 adds multi-range functions.
    72	
    73	**`remove_formula_deps` docstring is misleading about caller responsibilities.**
    74	
    75	The docstring says "those are revoked separately by the caller via `Graph::clear_outgoing` + `Graph::clear_range_deps_for_formula` (W5-50)." This is true for `extract_and_register_deps` but false for `on_clear_formula`, making the gap invisible from the doc. The docstring implies a contract that only one of two callers honors.
    76	
    77	### LOW
    78	
    79	- W5-50 commit message says "+4 unit tests" for `edges.rs` but the actual count is +5 (the functions `clear_outgoing_returns_prior_targets_and_empties`, `clear_outgoing_on_node_with_no_edges_is_empty`, `clear_outgoing_oob_panics`, `remove_back_pointer_removes_all_matching_entries`, `remove_back_pointer_preserves_others` = 5 new tests). Similarly "+7 unit tests" for `graph.rs` but the actual count is +8 (the 8 functions listed at lines 729-929). The total "+35" delta is correct; the per-file breakdown in the commit message is off by 1 in two files that cancel out.
    80	
    81	- The revision counter bumps for both `clear_outgoing` and `clear_range_deps_for_formula` even when no-op (empty edges / no range deps). Since these are called on every `extract_and_register_deps`, the revision counter advances 2 extra ticks per formula bind regardless of whether any state changed. This is harmless today but could confuse cache-validity checks that use `graph.revision()` as a cheapness proxy if they see spurious bumps.
    82	
    83	### Test gaps
    84	
    85	- **Missing: graph-edge staleness after `on_clear_formula`.** No test confirms that `graph.outgoing(cleared_formula)` is empty after clear, nor that `incoming` on the former deps no longer includes the cleared formula's node.
    86	- **Missing: stripe staleness after `on_clear_formula`.** No test confirms that `dependents_for_cell` no longer returns the cleared formula as a dependent after clear.
    87	- **Missing: supplemental duplicate-G scenario.** No test for a formula F with two ranges that overlap, both containing G, verifying that `supplemental[F]` either deduplicates G or that Tarjan handles the duplicate correctly.
    88	- **Missing: VEQ (value-equality short-circuit) interaction with supplemental.** Codex's watch list (decision doc, "Implementation watch list") explicitly named "VEQ behavior for range-dep formulas (Phase 3.8 short-circuit + supplemental edges must compose)." No test in W5-50 covers this. If VEQ short-circuits a formula that appears in the supplemental adjacency, the supplemental edge is correctly NOT followed (non-dirty target), so this is safe today — but the interaction is untested.
    89	- **Missing: `schedule_dirty` called with an already-empty dirty set.** The `schedule_dirty_with_empty_dirty_is_empty_schedule` test exists — good — but there is no test that schedule_dirty also clears `self.dirty` (it uses `drain()`, so it does), which could trip up callers that check `dirty_formulas()` after scheduling.
    90

    93	## W5-51 (trig batch)
    94	
    95	### HIGH
    96	None. The implementations are correct.
    97	
    98	### MEDIUM
    99	
   100	**Rustdoc title for `atan2` says `ATAN2(y, x)` — the wrong arg order.**
   101	
   102	File: `quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/scalar_fns.rs`, line 819:
   103	
   104	```rust
   105	/// `ATAN2(y, x)` — two-argument arc tangent. Returns radians in
   106	```
   107	
   108	The implementation correctly uses `args[0]` as `x` and `args[1]` as `y` (Excel order: `ATAN2(x_num, y_num)`), and the actual call is `y.atan2(x)`. The doc title inverts the argument labels, which is confusing to anyone reading the signature. The function body, the inline comment at line 843, and the test `atan2_basic_quadrants` are all correct. This is a documentation-only error but could mislead a future maintainer.
   109	
   110	### LOW
   111	
   112	- The ATAN2 doc says "Excel argument order is `ATAN2(x, y)` historically — but the W3C / OOXML standard and modern Excel use `ATAN2(x_num, y_num)`." This framing implies there was a historical inversion, but Excel has always used `ATAN2(x_num, y_num)`. The "historically" qualifier is misleading. Minor.
   113	- ATAN2 arity error returns `ErrorValue::Value` for wrong arg count. Excel returns `#VALUE!` for this case — correct. But 0 args and 3+ args both return `#VALUE!` — Excel returns `#VALUE!` for 0 args and some versions error differently for 3+ args. The behavior is acceptable.
   114	- Blank coerces to 0.0 in the ATAN2 implementation (`NumericArg::Skip => 0.0`). This means `ATAN2(0, 0)` where both args are blank returns `#DIV/0!` — which matches the explicit zero-zero check. But `ATAN2(blank, 1)` returns `ATAN2(0, 1) = π/2`, not an error. Excel behavior here varies by version; this may be acceptable but is untested.
   115	
   116	### Test gaps
   117	
   118	- **Missing: trig with infinity input.** `SIN(f64::INFINITY)` → NaN → `sanitize_f64` → `#NUM!`. No test for infinity input to any trig function.
   119	- **Missing: negative zero input.** `TAN(-0.0)` — behavior is well-defined (returns -0.0) but untested. 
   120	- **Missing: ATAN2 with one blank arg.** `ATAN2(blank, 1)` is not tested.
   121	- **Missing: ASIN/ACOS with NaN input.** The domain check `!(-1.0..=1.0).contains(&n)` correctly rejects NaN (NaN is not contained in any range), returning `#NUM!`. This is the right behavior but it is not explicitly tested.
   122	- The commit claims the compat coverage script "unchanged at 40%." The matrix update flips one row to ✅ but adds no new rows to the 217-row matrix. Coverage is correctly unchanged. No concern.
   123	
   124	---
   125	
   126	## Cross-cutting concerns
   127	
   128	### CONCERN-1 (function-signature scope discovery)
   129	
   130	The W5-51 commit honestly declares the gap: "batch ordering revised to lead with the scalar-only work that fits the existing `ScalarFn = fn(&[Value]) -> Value` contract." The W5-49 decision doc's stop-condition list says "Phase 4.3 V2 actually requires FormulaRegion for function SEMANTICS (not just perf)" but this is framed around FormulaRegion (Option B), not the simpler `ScalarFn` arity problem. The actual blocker for SUMIF is not FormulaRegion — it's that the function dispatch contract cannot distinguish "this argument is a range reference" from "this argument is a scalar." This is a narrower infrastructure problem than FormulaRegion, and the W5-49 doc does not mention it.
   131	
   132	This is (a) a legitimate scope discovery and (b) a real doc gap. Defensible as (a) because W5-49 was about graph correctness, not function-call infrastructure. Not fully defensible as (b) because the implementation outline explicitly says "Phase 4.3 V2 (SUMIF/COUNTIF/lookup family) is gated on GAP-G-01 + GAP-G-03 shipping" without noting any function-infra prerequisite. A reader of the decision doc would expect V2 to start immediately after W5-50, and it cannot. The W5-49 doc should have noted that range-aware functions also need a function signature extension.
   133	
   134	Codex's review listed this as a stop condition using the FormulaRegion framing but implicitly assumed `fn(&[Value]) -> Value` could somehow carry range metadata without modification. That's a Codex miss too.
   135	
   136	### CONCERN-2 (determinism of build_range_supplemental)
   137	
   138	The brief's analysis is correct. `dirty_vec` is sorted before `build_range_supplemental` runs (line 952-953: `dirty_vec.sort()`). `dirty_addrs` is built from `dirty_vec` in sorted order. The inner `for &(g, ...) in &dirty_addrs` iterates in sorted order. So `supplemental[F]` ends up with G's in sorted-by-NodeId order. Tarjan looks up by key (not by HashMap iteration order), so HashMap randomization is irrelevant. Determinism holds end-to-end. No bug found.
   139	
   140	One subtle note: if F has multiple ranges (e.g., `SUM(A:A) + SUM(A:A)`), the outer loop over `ranges` iterates them in the order stored in `formula_to_range_deps`. That order is the insertion order from `register_range_dependency`, which is the AST walk order from `walk_plan_for_deps`. This is deterministic for the same plan. Confirmed.
   141	
   142	### CONCERN-3 (self-edge in supplemental)
   143	
   144	The self-edge behavior is correct and intentional. `A1 = SUM(A1:A10)` where A1 is at row 0, col 0: `range_contains_rowcol(A1:A10, 0, 0)` returns true (row 0 is in `start_row..=end_row`, col 0 is `start_col..=end_col`). So `supplemental[A1] = [A1]` (self-loop). Tarjan's self-loop detection at line 254-266 correctly checks `supp_self_loop` via the supplemental map. The test `supplemental_self_loop_is_cycled` (topo.rs line 567) and `self_range_cycle_via_supplemental` (calcgraph_session.rs line 2313) confirm this. No bug.
   145	
   146	Excel would also treat `=SUM(A1:A10)` at cell A1 as a circular reference. Behavior is correct.
   147	
   148	### CONCERN-4 (cross-sheet range deps)
   149	
   150	The `build_range_supplemental` code resolves the range sheet with `Graph::range_ref_sheet(range).unwrap_or(fs)` (F's sheet) and then checks `gs == resolved_sheet`. For intra-sheet ranges (where `sheet: None` resolves to F's sheet), only G's on F's sheet are included. For explicitly cross-sheet ranges (where `sheet: Some(other)` is set), only G's on that other sheet would be included. This is structurally correct.
   151	
   152	However, as noted in the brief: Phase 3.3 sets `range.sheet = Some(_)` always during binding, so the `unwrap_or(fs)` fallback is currently unreachable. The future cross-sheet case (Phase 4.6) will work correctly if the binder sets `sheet: Some(target_sheet)` for cross-sheet refs. The one risk: if a future binder sets `sheet: None` for cross-sheet refs and relies on context, the supplemental builder would incorrectly scope G's to F's sheet. This is a latent risk, not a current bug.
   153	
   154	### CONCERN-5 (W5-51 trig edge cases not covered)
   155	
   156	The brief's analysis is accurate:
   157	- `TAN(FRAC_PI_2)`: test asserts `v > 1e10`. Actual value is `f64::tan(std::f64::consts::FRAC_PI_2)` = approximately `1.633e16`. The threshold `1e10` is loose but correct.
   158	- `SIN(INFINITY)` → NaN → `#NUM!`: untested (confirmed missing, see Test gaps above).
   159	- Negative zero: untested.
   160	- NaN through ASIN domain check: `!(-1.0..=1.0).contains(&NaN)` = true → returns `#NUM!` before calling `f64::asin`. Correct, untested.
   161	- ATAN2 blank arg: untested.
   162	
   163	### CONCERN-6 (perf of clear paths on first-bind)
   164	
   165	Confirmed cheap. On first-time bind of a formula node:
   166	- `edges.clear_outgoing(formula_node)`: `std::mem::take(&mut self.outgoing[idx])` on an empty `Vec<NodeId>` → returns empty vec → zero iterations over drained list → zero back-pointer removals. One revision bump.
   167	- `stripes.clear_for_formula(formula_node)`: `formula_to_stripe_keys.remove(&formula_node)` → HashMap remove of absent key → O(1) no-op. Zero stripe iterations.
   168	
   169	No perf concern for initial load path. The revision counter bumps twice per formula bind (once per clear call), which is the only overhead.
   170	
   171	### CONCERN-7 (cargo audit re-run)
   172	
   173	The W5-51 commit claims "cargo audit ✓ (1 pre-existing allowed warning)." W5-51 added no new dependencies (only `scalar_fns.rs`, `registry.rs`, and `excel-matrix.md` were changed). The cargo audit status is plausible as unchanged from W5-50. Cannot independently verify in this sandbox (no `cargo` in PATH), but there is no reason to doubt the claim given no Cargo.toml change.
   174	
   175	---
   176	
   177	## Recommended follow-up (W5-52 fix scope)
   178	
   179	### HIGH issues that MUST be fixed
   180	
   181	1. **`on_clear_formula` must call `graph.clear_outgoing(node)` and `graph.clear_range_deps_for_formula(node)`.** Add both calls after `remove_formula_deps(node)` at line 724 in `calcgraph_session.rs`. Add a regression test: set a formula with a range dep and a cell dep, clear it, then verify `graph.outgoing(node).is_empty()` and `graph.dependents_for_cell(...)` no longer returns the cleared node.
   182	
   183	### MEDIUM issues worth fixing
   184	
   185	2. **Deduplicate G in `build_range_supplemental`.** Add a `HashSet<NodeId>` per F inside the supplemental builder (or a post-pass `dedup` on each Vec) to prevent the same G from being pushed multiple times when F has overlapping ranges. Or, at minimum, add a test documenting the current behavior.
   186	
   187	3. **Fix the `atan2` rustdoc title.** Change `` `ATAN2(y, x)` `` to `` `ATAN2(x_num, y_num)` `` to match Excel's argument label convention and the implementation.
   188

   190	
   191	### LOW issues to defer or document
   192

   194

   196

     1	//! Function registry — name → implementation dispatch.
     2	//!
     3	//! Names are stored uppercase per Excel canon. Lookup is case-insensitive: the caller
     4	//! can pass `"sum"`, `"SUM"`, or `"Sum"` and get the same function.
     5	
     6	use std::collections::HashMap;
     7	
     8	use ql_types::Value;
     9	
    10	use crate::range_aware_fns::RangeAwareFn;
    11	use crate::{range_fns, scalar_fns, volatile};
    12	
    13	/// Function signature: pre-evaluated args → result Value.
    14	pub type ScalarFn = fn(&[Value]) -> Value;
    15	
    16	/// Phase 0 function registry. Built by `default_registry()` with the W4-4 function set.
    17	///
    18	/// W5-53 adds a parallel `range_aware_fns` table for functions that
    19	/// need per-argument range-vs-scalar metadata (SUMIF, COUNTIF, etc.).
    20	/// Lookup checks the range-aware table first; falls back to the
    21	/// scalar table. A single name MUST NOT be registered in both
    22	/// (registration panics if there's a conflict).
    23	#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
    24	pub struct FunctionRegistry {
    25	    fns: HashMap<&'static str, ScalarFn>,
    26	    range_aware_fns: HashMap<&'static str, RangeAwareFn>,
    27	}
    28	
    29	impl Default for FunctionRegistry {
    30	    fn default() -> Self {
    31	        Self::new()
    32	    }
    33	}
    34	
    35	impl FunctionRegistry {
    36	    /// Empty registry — caller adds functions via `register`. Use `default_registry()`
    37	    /// for the Phase 0 built-in set.
    38	    pub fn new() -> Self {
    39	        Self {
    40	            fns: HashMap::new(),
    41	            range_aware_fns: HashMap::new(),
    42	        }
    43	    }
    44	
    45	    /// Register a function under `name`.
    46	    ///
    47	    /// Phase 2A.7 audit M5: `name` MUST already be canonical upper-case. This is
    48	    /// asserted at registration time so a stray `register("sum", ...)` doesn't
    49	    /// silently create an unreachable entry (lookups uppercase the query, so a
    50	    /// lower-case key would never be found). Duplicate registrations also
    51	    /// panic — silent override would let a typo replace a built-in with a buggy
    52	    /// shim. Phase 2 has a closed default function set; if dynamic registration
    53	    /// ever becomes a real use case, swap this for `Result<(), RegisterError>`.
    54	    pub fn register(&mut self, name: &'static str, f: ScalarFn) {
    55	        assert!(
    56	            !name.is_empty(),
    57	            "FunctionRegistry::register: name must not be empty"
    58	        );
    59	        assert!(
    60	            name.bytes().all(|b| !b.is_ascii_lowercase()),
    61	            "FunctionRegistry::register: name {name:?} must be canonical upper-case; \
    62	             lookups uppercase the query, so a lower-case key is unreachable"
    63	        );
    64	        let prior = self.fns.insert(name, f);
    65	        assert!(
    66	            prior.is_none(),
    67	            "FunctionRegistry::register: duplicate registration for {name:?} — \
    68	             silent override would let a typo replace a built-in"
    69	        );
    70	    }
    71	
    72	    /// W5-53: register a range-aware function under `name`. Same
    73	    /// canonical-uppercase requirement + duplicate panic as
    74	    /// `register`. Cross-table name collision also panics — a name
    75	    /// cannot live in both `fns` and `range_aware_fns`.
    76	    pub fn register_range_aware(&mut self, name: &'static str, f: RangeAwareFn) {
    77	        assert!(
    78	            !name.is_empty(),
    79	            "FunctionRegistry::register_range_aware: name must not be empty"
    80	        );
    81	        assert!(
    82	            name.bytes().all(|b| !b.is_ascii_lowercase()),
    83	            "FunctionRegistry::register_range_aware: name {name:?} must be canonical \
    84	             upper-case"
    85	        );
    86	        assert!(
    87	            !self.fns.contains_key(name),
    88	            "FunctionRegistry::register_range_aware: {name:?} is already registered \
    89	             as a scalar function; cannot register in both tables"
    90	        );
    91	        let prior = self.range_aware_fns.insert(name, f);
    92	        assert!(
    93	            prior.is_none(),
    94	            "FunctionRegistry::register_range_aware: duplicate registration for {name:?}"
    95	        );
    96	    }
    97	
    98	    /// Case-insensitive lookup. Returns `None` if not registered.
    99	    pub fn lookup(&self, name: &str) -> Option<ScalarFn> {
   100	        // Allocate a single uppercase key for the lookup; the registry holds &'static
   101	        // uppercase names, so we compare on uppercase form.
   102	        let upper = name.to_ascii_uppercase();
   103	        self.fns.get(upper.as_str()).copied()
   104	    }
   105	
   106	    /// W5-53: case-insensitive lookup in the range-aware table.
   107	    /// Callers should check this BEFORE `lookup` — if a function is
   108	    /// range-aware, the dispatch must construct `Vec<FnArg>` rather
   109	    /// than flattening to `Vec<Value>`.
   110	    pub fn lookup_range_aware(&self, name: &str) -> Option<RangeAwareFn> {
   111	        let upper = name.to_ascii_uppercase();
   112	        self.range_aware_fns.get(upper.as_str()).copied()
   113	    }
   114	
   115	    pub fn names(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = &&'static str> {
   116	        self.fns.keys()
   117	    }
   118	
   119	    pub fn len(&self) -> usize {
   120	        self.fns.len() + self.range_aware_fns.len()
   121	    }
   122	
   123	    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
   124	        self.fns.is_empty() && self.range_aware_fns.is_empty()
   125	    }
   126	}
   127	
   128	/// Phase 0 default registry with the W4-4 built-in function set (22 functions).

     1	//! `ExprPlan` — execution-ready intermediate representation.
     2	//!
     3	//! Sits between `ql_formula_syntax::Expr` (parser output, may carry `Option<SheetId>` for
     4	//! unresolved same-sheet refs) and the kernel dispatch in `scalar.rs` / `simd.rs` (W4-2).
     5	//! `bind()` walks the Expr tree and resolves every `CellAddr.sheet: Option<SheetId>` to a
     6	//! concrete `SheetId` based on the formula's owning sheet.
     7	//!
     8	//! The plan is "execution-ready" but still tree-shaped — it does NOT yet flatten the
     9	//! expression into a SIMD-friendly opcode stream. That's the role of `kernel_simd` in W4-2,
    10	//! which lowers `ExprPlan::Binary { op: Mul, lhs: CellRef, rhs: Number }` into a
    11	//! `multiversion`-dispatched `Float64Array * f64` call.
    12	//!
    13	//! ## Phase 0 scope
    14	//!
    15	//! Subset of `Expr` variants covered:
    16	//! - `Number(f64)` — numeric literal
    17	//! - `Bool(bool)` — boolean literal
    18	//! - `String(Arc<str>)` — text literal (binds 1:1)
    19	//! - `CellRef { sheet, row, col, abs_col, abs_row }` — sheet RESOLVED to concrete SheetId
    20	//! - `Binary { op, lhs, rhs }` — arithmetic + concat + comparison
    21	//! - `Unary { op, operand }` — unary minus / plus / percent
    22	//!
    23	//! Deferred (will surface in W4-4 ql-functions integration):
    24	//! - `RangeRef` — single range used inside a Function call (e.g. `SUM(A:A)`)
    25	//! - `Function { name, args }` — registry dispatch
    26	//! - `Array`, `Spill` — Phase 3+ dynamic arrays
    27	
    28	use std::sync::Arc;
    29	
    30	use ql_formula_syntax::{Expr, Operator};
    31	use ql_types::{ColId, ErrorValue, Range, RowId, SheetId};
    32	
    33	/// Bound, execution-ready expression. Sheet refs are concrete.
    34	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
    35	pub enum ExprPlan {
    36	    Number(f64),
    37	    Bool(bool),
    38	    String(Arc<str>),
    39	    CellRef {
    40	        sheet: SheetId,
    41	        row: RowId,
    42	        col: ColId,
    43	        abs_col: bool,
    44	        abs_row: bool,
    45	    },
    46	    Binary {
    47	        op: Operator,
    48	        lhs: Box<ExprPlan>,
    49	        rhs: Box<ExprPlan>,
    50	    },
    51	    Unary {
    52	        op: Operator,
    53	        operand: Box<ExprPlan>,
    54	    },
    55	    /// Function call. Args are bound recursively. Phase 0 W4-5: name is preserved
    56	    /// verbatim from the AST (Excel-canonical uppercase normalization happens at the
    57	    /// parser level — W4-5 expects already-uppercased names from the binder). The
    58	    /// scalar evaluator looks up the name in a `FunctionRegistry` at evaluation time.
    59	    Function {
    60	        name: Arc<str>,
    61	        args: Vec<ExprPlan>,
    62	    },
    63	    /// Phase 2B.4 (2026-05-12): a `NameRef` resolving to a `NamedTarget::Range`
    64	    /// in an aggregate-argument position (e.g. the arg of `SUM`, `AVERAGE`,
    65	    /// `COUNT`). Carries the canonical name + the resolved Range. The scalar
    66	    /// evaluator currently rejects this with `Value::Error(ErrorValue::Calc)`
    67	    /// because aggregate-range evaluation is Engine Phase 3.6 work; the bind
    68	    /// shape lands now so the IDE / op-log surfaces are stable when Phase 3.6
    69	    /// flips the eval on.
    70	    ///
    71	    /// In scalar (non-aggregate) positions, the same `NameRef` produces
    72	    /// [`BindError::NamedRangeInScalarContext`] instead of binding to this
    73	    /// variant.
    74	    AggregateNameRef {
    75	        /// Canonical (uppercase) name as it appears in the NameTable.
    76	        name: Arc<str>,
    77	        /// Pre-resolved range payload. Phase 3.6 evaluator reads from this
    78	        /// directly rather than re-looking-up the name.
    79	        range: Range,
    80	    },
    81	}
    82	
    83	/// Error during binding. Phase 2A.1 added `UnresolvedName`; Phase 2A.6 added
    84	/// `NamedTargetIs{Blank,Error}`; Phase 2A.11 audit M16 switched to
    85	/// `thiserror::Error` for consistency with the rest of the error surface.
    86	/// Display strings are now user-facing (suitable for IDE diagnostics).
    87	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, thiserror::Error)]
    88	pub enum BindError {
    89	    /// The Expr contains a variant not supported in this build (e.g. RangeRef
    90	    /// outside a Function context, Array literal, Spill anchor, NamedTarget::Range
    91	    /// — all Engine Phase 4 work per `docs/MASTER-PLAN.md`).
    92	    #[error("unsupported expression variant: {0}")]
    93	    UnsupportedVariant(&'static str),
    94	    /// A `NameRef` was used but the name isn't registered in the active `NameTable`.
    95	    /// Phase 2A.1: equivalent to Excel's `#NAME?` but surfaced at bind time rather
    96	    /// than as a runtime Error value, so the IDE can highlight the offending token
    97	    /// before evaluation.
    98	    #[error("unresolved name {0:?}")]
    99	    UnresolvedName(Arc<str>),
   100	    /// A `NameRef` resolved to a `NamedTarget::Constant(Value::Blank)`. Phase 2A.6
   101	    /// audit M2: previously this was silently coerced to `Text("")`, producing
   102	    /// nonsense in arithmetic contexts. Now surfaced loudly so callers fix the
   103	    /// data binding.
   104	    #[error("named constant {0:?} is blank; cannot be used in this context")]
   105	    NamedTargetIsBlank(Arc<str>),
   106	    /// A `NameRef` resolved to a `NamedTarget::Constant(Value::Error(_))`. Phase 2A.6
   107	    /// audit M3: previously silently mapped to `UnresolvedName` (wrong error class —
   108	    /// the name IS resolved, just to an error value). Carries the underlying
   109	    /// `ErrorValue` so the IDE can echo `#DIV/0!` / `#REF!` etc. accurately.
   110	    // Phase 2A.13 audit cycle-3 M1: `{1}` (Display) instead of `{1:?}` (Debug).
   111	    // `ErrorValue` has a `Display` impl that renders the canonical Excel sigil
   112	    // (`#DIV/0!` etc.); the prior debug formatter leaked Rust enum identifiers
   113	    // ("DivZero") into IDE diagnostics — the same mistake WS-4 was supposed to
   114	    // close everywhere. Note `{0:?}` is kept deliberately on the name field
   115	    // so the Arc<str> is rendered with surrounding quotes for visual clarity
   116	    // ("named constant \"X\" ..." vs the bare-string ambiguous "named constant X").
   117	    #[error("named constant {0:?} holds an error value: {1}")]
   118	    NamedTargetIsError(Arc<str>, ErrorValue),
   119	    /// Phase 2B.4 (2026-05-12): a `NameRef` resolving to a `NamedTarget::Range`
   120	    /// appeared in a scalar-context position (e.g. `=Sales + 1` instead of
   121	    /// `=SUM(Sales)`). Excel returns `#VALUE!` at evaluation; we surface it at
   122	    /// bind so the IDE highlights the offending token before any state writes.
   123	    /// Distinct from the generic `UnsupportedVariant` so callers can render a
   124	    /// specific "this name is a range; use it inside an aggregate function"
   125	    /// hint.
   126	    #[error(
   127	        "named range {0:?} cannot be used in this scalar position; \
   128	         wrap it in an aggregate function such as SUM, AVERAGE, COUNT, MIN, MAX"
   129	    )]
   130	    NamedRangeInScalarContext(Arc<str>),
   131	    /// Phase 2B.4 (2026-05-12): a `NameRef` resolving to a
   132	    /// `NamedTarget::Formula` in a scalar-context position. Named formulas
   133	    /// (`Profit = Revenue - Costs`) are Engine Phase 4 work; this distinct
   134	    /// variant tells the IDE / caller that the name is recognized but the
   135	    /// named-formula feature itself is not yet shipped.
   136	    #[error(
   137	        "named formula {0:?} cannot be used in this position; \
   138	         named-formula resolution is Engine Phase 4 work"
   139	    )]
   140	    NamedFormulaUnsupported(Arc<str>),
   141	}
   142	
   143	/// Phase 2B.4 (2026-05-12): bind-time context for a sub-expression. Drives
   144	/// per-name-ref decisions about whether a `NamedTarget::Range` is acceptable
   145	/// (aggregate-arg) or surfaces as `BindError::NamedRangeInScalarContext`
   146	/// (scalar). Internal to the binder; not exposed in the public API yet.
   147	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
   148	enum BindContext {
   149	    /// Default. NameRefs resolving to ranges or formulas are rejected with
   150	    /// precise errors.
   151	    Scalar,
   152	    /// Inside the argument list of an aggregate function (SUM, AVERAGE,
   153	    /// MIN, MAX, COUNT, etc. — see `is_aggregate_function`). NameRefs
   154	    /// resolving to ranges bind to `ExprPlan::AggregateNameRef`; named
   155	    /// formulas still error (Engine Phase 4 work).
   156	    AggregateArg,
   157	}
   158	
   159	/// Phase 2B.4 (2026-05-12): hardcoded list of functions whose arguments
   160	/// accept aggregate-range inputs. Tracked here as a V0 stub; Engine Phase
   161	/// 4.3 (function library expansion) replaces this with per-function
   162	/// metadata on `FunctionRegistry`. The names below MUST match the
   163	/// canonical uppercase form the parser emits.
   164	///
   165	/// Aggregate detection is binary in V0 (all args are aggregate or no args
   166	/// are). Per-arg-position decisions (e.g. IF's then/else accept arrays in
   167	/// some contexts) land alongside Engine Phase 4.7 array formulas.
   168	pub(crate) fn is_aggregate_function(name: &str) -> bool {
   169	    // Phase 2B.7 audit (correctness M1): MEDIAN removed — it was listed
   170	    // here but not registered in `ql_functions::default_registry`. Keeping
   171	    // the list synced with the registry is a hard rule; the
   172	    // `is_aggregate_function_lists_only_registered_aggregates` test pins
   173	    // it. When Engine Phase 4.3 expands the function library, the metadata
   174	    // moves to per-function FunctionRegistry attributes and this hardcoded
   175	    // matcher goes away entirely.
   176	    //
   177	    // W5-53 (GAP-F-05 closure): SUMIF + COUNTIF are NOT scalar aggregates
   178	    // (they return a scalar but take range + criteria) — they live in the
   179	    // parallel `range_aware_fns` table. But the binder uses this list to
   180	    // decide whether `NamedRangeInScalarContext` should fire on
   181	    // `=SUMIF(NamedRange, 5)`. Since SUMIF NEEDS a range arg, we add it
   182	    // here so the binder allows the AggregateNameRef in arg position. The
   183	    // eval-side dispatch routes correctly via `lookup_range_aware` first.
   184	    matches!(
   185	        name,
   186	        "SUM"
   187	            | "AVERAGE"
   188	            | "AVG"
   189	            | "COUNT"
   190	            | "COUNTA"
   191	            | "MIN"
   192	            | "MAX"
   193	            | "PRODUCT"
   194	            | "VAR"
   195	            | "VAR.S"
   196	            | "VAR.P"
   197	            | "STDEV"
   198	            | "STDEV.S"
   199	            | "STDEV.P"
   200	            // Range-aware (W5-53): NOT scalar aggregates, but the binder
   201	            // treats them as aggregate-context for arg binding so named-
   202	            // range args resolve to `AggregateNameRef`.
   203	            | "SUMIF"
   204	            | "COUNTIF"
   205	            // Range-aware lookup family (W5-54): same reason — table
   206	            // args are range references that must bind as
   207	            // AggregateNameRef so the eval-side dispatch can construct
   208	            // `FnArg::Range { values, rows, cols }`.
   209	            | "MATCH"
   210	            | "INDEX"
   211	            | "VLOOKUP"
   212	            | "HLOOKUP"
   213	            | "CHOOSE"
   214	            // Range-aware conditional-aggregate completion (W5-55).
   215	            | "AVERAGEIF"
   216	            | "SUMIFS"
   217	            | "COUNTIFS"
   218	            | "AVERAGEIFS"
   219	            | "SUMPRODUCT"
   220	            // Range-aware stats family (W5-58, FN4-01 closure).
   221	            | "LARGE"
   222	            | "SMALL"
   223	            | "RANK"
   224	            | "RANK.EQ"
   225	            | "MEDIAN"
   226	            | "MODE"
   227	            | "MODE.SNGL"
   228	    )
   229	}
   230	
   231	/// Resolve an Expr against an owning sheet, producing an ExprPlan. **No name
   232	/// resolution** — any `Expr::NameRef` produces `BindError::UnresolvedName`.
   233	/// Callers that need named-range support should use `bind_with_names` and pass
   234	/// a `&NameTable`.
   235	///
   236	/// `owning_sheet` is the sheet the formula lives on. AST `CellAddr.sheet` of `None`
   237	/// means "this sheet"; `Some(s)` means an explicit sheet ref (Phase 3+ shipping the
   238	/// parser surface; Phase 0/1 always has `None` from the lexer).
   239	pub fn bind(expr: &Expr, owning_sheet: SheetId) -> Result<ExprPlan, BindError> {
   240	    bind_with_names(expr, owning_sheet, &EmptyNameLookup)

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	//! Range-aware function dispatch (W5-53, GAP-F-05 closure).
     2	//!
     3	//! The base `ScalarFn = fn(&[Value]) -> Value` contract can't carry
     4	//! per-argument range-vs-scalar metadata. Functions like SUMIF /
     5	//! COUNTIF / VLOOKUP need to distinguish "this argument is a range
     6	//! (criteria_range, sum_range, lookup_table)" from "this argument is
     7	//! a single value (criteria, lookup_value)". Flattening every range
     8	//! into a `Vec<Value>` and concatenating before dispatch loses that
     9	//! structure.
    10	//!
    11	//! Phase 4.3 V2 (W5-53) introduces a parallel function-table tier
    12	//! that preserves the shape:
    13	//!
    14	//! ```rust,ignore
    15	//! pub enum FnArg {
    16	//!     Scalar(Value),
    17	//!     Range(Vec<Value>),
    18	//! }
    19	//! pub type RangeAwareFn = fn(&[FnArg]) -> Value;
    20	//! ```
    21	//!
    22	//! The `eval_scalar_with_cache` dispatch in `ql-exec::scalar` checks
    23	//! the range-aware table first; if a function is registered there,
    24	//! args are built as `Vec<FnArg>` (AggregateNameRef → `Range`, other
    25	//! plans → `Scalar(evaluated)`). Otherwise it falls through to the
    26	//! existing `ScalarFn` path with `Vec<Value>` flattening.
    27	//!
    28	//! Migration of existing functions to `RangeAwareFn` is NOT required
    29	//! — they keep working under `ScalarFn`. Only new functions that
    30	//! genuinely need per-arg range distinction use the new tier.
    31	
    32	use ql_types::Value;
    33	
    34	/// A single argument to a range-aware function. `Scalar` carries a
    35	/// pre-evaluated `Value`; `Range` carries the flat row-major
    36	/// iteration of a range reference (per `CellEnv::read_range`) PLUS
    37	/// its 2D shape (rows × cols).
    38	///
    39	/// Shape is required for 2D-addressing functions like VLOOKUP /
    40	/// HLOOKUP / INDEX, which compute `values[row * cols + col]`. 1D
    41	/// consumers (SUMIF, COUNTIF) ignore shape and iterate `values`
    42	/// flat — `rows * cols == values.len()` is the invariant the eval
    43	/// dispatch maintains.
    44	///
    45	/// `values` is a `Vec<Value>` rather than a slice because the
    46	/// producer (eval) owns the buffer. For very large ranges (whole-
    47	/// column refs over millions of cells) this is the same cost as the
    48	/// existing flattening path; range-aware functions get both the
    49	/// flat data and the structure.
    50	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
    51	pub enum FnArg {
    52	    Scalar(Value),
    53	    Range {
    54	        values: Vec<Value>,
    55	        rows: usize,
    56	        cols: usize,
    57	    },
    58	}
    59	
    60	impl FnArg {
    61	    /// Construct a 1D `Range` (1 row × N cols, or N rows × 1 col —
    62	    /// caller decides which axis). Convenience for tests + 1D
    63	    /// consumers; uses `rows = 1, cols = values.len()` by default.
    64	    /// Production callers should construct the variant directly so
    65	    /// the shape reflects the source range.
    66	    #[cfg(test)]
    67	    pub fn range_1d(values: Vec<Value>) -> Self {
    68	        let cols = values.len();
    69	        FnArg::Range {
    70	            values,
    71	            rows: 1,
    72	            cols,
    73	        }
    74	    }
    75	
    76	    /// Construct a 2D `Range` with explicit shape.
    77	    #[cfg(test)]
    78	    pub fn range_2d(values: Vec<Value>, rows: usize, cols: usize) -> Self {
    79	        debug_assert_eq!(rows * cols, values.len(), "shape mismatch");
    80	        FnArg::Range { values, rows, cols }
    81	    }
    82	
    83	    /// Convenience for tests + scalar-only call sites: returns the
    84	    /// inner `Value` if this is a scalar arg, panics otherwise.
    85	    /// Production code should `match` on the variant explicitly.
    86	    #[cfg(test)]
    87	    pub fn unwrap_scalar(&self) -> &Value {
    88	        match self {
    89	            FnArg::Scalar(v) => v,
    90	            FnArg::Range { .. } => panic!("FnArg::unwrap_scalar called on a Range arg"),
    91	        }
    92	    }
    93	
    94	    /// Convenience for tests: returns the inner values if Range,
    95	    /// panics otherwise.
    96	    #[cfg(test)]
    97	    pub fn unwrap_range(&self) -> &[Value] {
    98	        match self {
    99	            FnArg::Range { values, .. } => values.as_slice(),
   100	            FnArg::Scalar(_) => panic!("FnArg::unwrap_range called on a Scalar arg"),
   101	        }
   102	    }
   103	}
   104	
   105	/// Signature for range-aware built-in functions. The evaluator
   106	/// constructs the arg list with the correct `FnArg` variant per
   107	/// position before dispatching.
   108	pub type RangeAwareFn = fn(&[FnArg]) -> Value;
