     1	//! Scalar evaluator — `eval_scalar(plan, env) -> Value`.
     2	//!
     3	//! Per-cell evaluation path. Used for:
     4	//! - Single-cell formulas (`=A1+B1` in cell C1).
     5	//! - Per-cell fallback inside a FormulaRegion when SIMD isn't applicable (text, error,
     6	//!   mixed types, non-arithmetic ops, division — see lower.rs H5 routing).
     7	//! - Tests, which is most of the W4-1 coverage.
     8	//!
     9	//! The SIMD hot path for OG-02 lives in `simd.rs` and bypasses this entirely —
    10	//! it consumes Arrow Float64Array slices directly via `multiversion`-dispatched
    11	//! kernels.
    12	//!
    13	//! ## Excel binary arithmetic semantics (Phase 2A.9 audit M1 — lenient coercion)
    14	//!
    15	//! For binary operators `+ - * / ^`:
    16	//! - If either operand is an `Error`, propagate that error (left operand wins).
    17	//! - Coerce both operands to f64 via `to_number_lenient` (Number → self;
    18	//!   Bool → 0/1; Blank → 0; Text that parses as a number → that number; Text
    19	//!   that doesn't parse → `#VALUE!`; Error → propagate). Phase 2A.9 audit M1
    20	//!   replaced the prior strict path that rejected ALL text.
    21	//! - Apply the f64 op. Sanitize result via `sanitize_f64` (NaN/Inf → `#NUM!`).
    22	//! - Special cases (Phase 2A.9 audit M3 / 2A.13 audit M3 companion):
    23	//!   - Division by zero → `#DIV/0!`.
    24	//!   - Negative base ^ non-integer exponent → `#NUM!`.
    25	//!   - `0^0` → `#NUM!`.
    26	//!   - `0^-n` (n > 0) → `#DIV/0!` (mathematically `1/0^n`).
    27	//!
    28	//! For `&` (concat): coerce both to text via `to_text_for_formula`; concat. Errors
    29	//! propagate.
    30	//!
    31	//! Comparison operators (`=`, `<>`, `<`, `>`, `<=`, `>=`) follow Excel-canon
    32	//! cross-type ordering (Phase 2A.9 audit M2): same-type pairs compare per
    33	//! their natural ordering; mixed-type pairs use type rank
    34	//! (`Number < Text < Boolean`). `Blank` coerces to `0` vs Number and `""`
    35	//! vs Text; for `Blank vs Boolean` the type-rank fallthrough applies (Blank
    36	//! ranks as Number, so `Blank < TRUE`). See `compare_values_excel`.
    37	//!
    38	//! Unary `-x` negates; `+x` no-ops; `x%` divides by 100.
    39	
    40	use ql_formula_syntax::Operator;
    41	use ql_functions::FunctionRegistry;
    42	use ql_types::{coercion, ErrorValue, Value};
    43	
    44	use crate::aggregate_cache::{AggregateCache, NoAggregateCache};
    45	use crate::env::CellEnv;
    46	use crate::plan::ExprPlan;
    47	
    48	/// Evaluate an `ExprPlan` against `env` to produce a `Value`. Total function — never
    49	/// panics on Excel semantics (NaN, div-by-zero, etc.). Panics only on internal
    50	/// programmer errors (e.g. malformed ExprPlan that bind() couldn't produce).
    51	///
    52	/// `Function` variants return `#NAME?` (`ErrorValue::Name`). To dispatch through a
    53	/// registry, use `eval_scalar_with_registry` instead.
    54	pub fn eval_scalar<E: CellEnv>(plan: &ExprPlan, env: &E) -> Value {
    55	    match plan {
    56	        ExprPlan::Number(n) => Value::number(*n),
    57	        ExprPlan::Bool(b) => Value::Boolean(*b),
    58	        ExprPlan::String(s) => Value::Text(s.clone()),
    59	        ExprPlan::CellRef {
    60	            sheet, row, col, ..
    61	        } => env.read_cell(*sheet, *row, *col),
    62	        ExprPlan::Binary { op, lhs, rhs } => {
    63	            let l = eval_scalar(lhs, env);
    64	            let r = eval_scalar(rhs, env);
    65	            eval_binary(*op, l, r)
    66	        }
    67	        ExprPlan::Unary { op, operand } => {
    68	            let v = eval_scalar(operand, env);
    69	            eval_unary(*op, v)
    70	        }
    71	        ExprPlan::Function { .. } => Value::Error(ErrorValue::Name),
    72	        // Phase 2B.4 (2026-05-12): the bind shape exists but aggregate-range
    73	        // evaluation lands in Engine Phase 3.6. Surface a clean Excel-canon
    74	        // `#CALC!` so the cell shows a recognizable error sigil rather than
    75	        // silently producing wrong values. The enclosing aggregate function
    76	        // (SUM, AVERAGE, ...) sees the error and propagates per
    77	        // Excel's left-error-wins rule.
    78	        ExprPlan::AggregateNameRef { .. } => Value::Error(ErrorValue::Calc),
    79	    }
    80	}
    81	
    82	/// Like `eval_scalar` but dispatches `Function` variants through a `FunctionRegistry`.
    83	/// Unknown function names return `#NAME?`. Args are evaluated left-to-right with this
    84	/// same function (recursive); error-args propagate naturally through the registered
    85	/// function's logic.
    86	pub fn eval_scalar_with_registry<E: CellEnv>(
    87	    plan: &ExprPlan,
    88	    env: &E,
    89	    registry: &FunctionRegistry,
    90	) -> Value {
    91	    // Phase 3.6: legacy entry point — no aggregate cache. Wraps the cached
    92	    // path with `NoAggregateCache` (zero-cost no-op impl). Callers that
    93	    // have a session-owned cache should call `eval_scalar_with_cache`.
    94	    eval_scalar_with_cache(plan, env, registry, &NoAggregateCache)
    95	}
    96	
    97	/// **Phase 3.6 (2026-05-12) — W5-39, AGG-3-01..04 entry point.** Like
    98	/// `eval_scalar_with_registry` but consults `cache` for aggregate-over-
    99	/// range evaluations. When the Function branch detects an aggregate fn
   100	/// with an `AggregateNameRef` arg, it:
   101	///
   102	/// 1. Looks up `(range, function_name)` in `cache`. Hit → returns the
   103	///    cached value without re-scanning the range.
   104	/// 2. Miss → materializes the range via `CellEnv::read_range`, calls
   105	///    the aggregator, stores the result in `cache`, returns it.
   106	///
   107	/// The cache is invalidated externally (via `CalcgraphSession::
   108	/// mark_dirty_from_cell_write` → `InMemAggregateCache::invalidate_at`)
   109	/// when a cell inside any cached range is written. The evaluator itself
   110	/// never invalidates — it only reads + stores.
   111	///
   112	/// Pass `&NoAggregateCache` for the legacy "always recompute" behavior.
   113	pub fn eval_scalar_with_cache<E: CellEnv>(
   114	    plan: &ExprPlan,
   115	    env: &E,
   116	    registry: &FunctionRegistry,
   117	    cache: &dyn AggregateCache,
   118	) -> Value {
   119	    match plan {
   120	        ExprPlan::Number(n) => Value::number(*n),
   121	        ExprPlan::Bool(b) => Value::Boolean(*b),
   122	        ExprPlan::String(s) => Value::Text(s.clone()),
   123	        ExprPlan::CellRef {
   124	            sheet, row, col, ..
   125	        } => env.read_cell(*sheet, *row, *col),
   126	        ExprPlan::Binary { op, lhs, rhs } => {
   127	            let l = eval_scalar_with_cache(lhs, env, registry, cache);
   128	            let r = eval_scalar_with_cache(rhs, env, registry, cache);
   129	            eval_binary(*op, l, r)
   130	        }
   131	        ExprPlan::Unary { op, operand } => {
   132	            let v = eval_scalar_with_cache(operand, env, registry, cache);
   133	            eval_unary(*op, v)
   134	        }
   135	        ExprPlan::Function { name, args } => {
   136	            // W5-53 (GAP-F-05 closure): check the range-aware table
   137	            // FIRST. Range-aware functions like SUMIF need per-arg
   138	            // range-vs-scalar metadata that the scalar `&[Value]`
   139	            // contract can't carry. If a function is registered as
   140	            // range-aware, build `Vec<FnArg>` with the correct
   141	            // variant per arg (`AggregateNameRef` → `Range(read_range)`,
   142	            // everything else → `Scalar(eval)`).
   143	            if let Some(raf) = registry.lookup_range_aware(name) {
   144	                use ql_functions::FnArg;
   145	                let mut fn_args: Vec<FnArg> = Vec::with_capacity(args.len());
   146	                for a in args {
   147	                    match a {
   148	                        ExprPlan::AggregateNameRef { range, .. } => {
   149	                            // W5-54: shape-aware Range so VLOOKUP /
   150	                            // HLOOKUP / INDEX can address by (row,
   151	                            // col). SUMIF / COUNTIF ignore shape.
   152	                            let (values, rows, cols) = env.read_range_with_shape(*range);
   153	                            fn_args.push(FnArg::Range { values, rows, cols });
   154	                        }
   155	                        other => {
   156	                            fn_args.push(FnArg::Scalar(eval_scalar_with_cache(
   157	                                other, env, registry, cache,
   158	                            )));
   159	                        }
   160	                    }
   161	                }
   162	                return raf(&fn_args);
   163	            }
   164	            match registry.lookup(name) {
   165	                Some(f) => {
   166	                    // Phase 3.6 (2026-05-12) — AGG-3-04 correctness + AGG-
   167	                    // 3-01 cache hit path. Detect aggregate-over-named-range
   168	                    // and route through the cache. The cache key is `(range,
   169	                    // function_name)` — the EXACT call shape, not just the
   170	                    // range — so `SUM(Sales)` and `AVERAGE(Sales)` are
   171	                    // separate entries.
   172	                    //
   173	                    // Single-AggregateNameRef-arg fast path: most aggregates
   174	                    // are called with a single named range (`=SUM(Sales)`).
   175	                    // We special-case that for cache use. Multi-range
   176	                    // aggregates (`SUM(Sales, Discount)`) fall back to no-
   177	                    // cache (compute on every call) — V1 limitation.
   178	                    let single_range_arg = match args.as_slice() {
   179	                        [ExprPlan::AggregateNameRef { range, .. }] => Some(*range),
   180	                        _ => None,
   181	                    };
   182	                    if let Some(range) = single_range_arg {
   183	                        if crate::plan::is_aggregate_function(name) {
   184	                            if let Some(cached) = cache.lookup_aggregate(range, name) {
   185	                                return cached;
   186	                            }
   187	                            let flat = env.read_range(range);
   188	                            let v = f(&flat);
   189	                            // Don't cache `Value::Error(_)` results — they
   190	                            // often signal env-level issues (missing sheet
   191	                            // → #REF!, empty range AVERAGE → #DIV/0!) that
   192	                            // a follow-up edit would resolve; better to
   193	                            // recompute than serve a stale error.
   194	                            if !matches!(v, Value::Error(_)) {
   195	                                cache.store_aggregate(range, name, v.clone());
   196	                            }
   197	                            return v;
   198	                        }
   199	                    }
   200	                    let has_range_arg = args
   201	                        .iter()
   202	                        .any(|a| matches!(a, ExprPlan::AggregateNameRef { .. }));
   203	                    if has_range_arg && crate::plan::is_aggregate_function(name) {
   204	                        // Multi-range or mixed aggregate args: materialize
   205	                        // every range and call the function. No cache use
   206	                        // (V2 may add multi-range cache keys).
   207	                        let mut flat: Vec<Value> = Vec::new();
   208	                        for a in args {
   209	                            match a {
   210	                                ExprPlan::AggregateNameRef { range, .. } => {
   211	                                    flat.extend(env.read_range(*range));
   212	                                }
   213	                                other => {
   214	                                    flat.push(eval_scalar_with_cache(other, env, registry, cache));
   215	                                }
   216	                            }
   217	                        }
   218	                        f(&flat)
   219	                    } else {
   220	                        let evaluated: Vec<Value> = args
   221	                            .iter()
   222	                            .map(|a| eval_scalar_with_cache(a, env, registry, cache))
   223	                            .collect();
   224	                        f(&evaluated)
   225	                    }
   226	                }
   227	                None => Value::Error(ErrorValue::Name),
   228	            }
   229	        }
   230	        // `AggregateNameRef` outside a Function context — the binder is
   231	        // supposed to surface `BindError::NamedRangeInScalarContext`
   232	        // before we ever evaluate. Defensive fallback: `#CALC!`.
   233	        ExprPlan::AggregateNameRef { .. } => Value::Error(ErrorValue::Calc),
   234	    }
   235	}
   236	
   237	fn eval_binary(op: Operator, lhs: Value, rhs: Value) -> Value {
   238	    // Error propagation comes first — Excel's left-error-wins rule.
   239	    if let Value::Error(e) = lhs {
   240	        return Value::Error(e);
   241	    }
   242	    if let Value::Error(e) = rhs {
   243	        return Value::Error(e);
   244	    }
   245	
   246	    match op {
   247	        Operator::Plus | Operator::Minus | Operator::Mul | Operator::Div | Operator::Pow => {
   248	            eval_arithmetic(op, &lhs, &rhs)
   249	        }
   250	        Operator::Concat => eval_concat(&lhs, &rhs),
   251	        Operator::Eq
   252	        | Operator::Neq
   253	        | Operator::Lt
   254	        | Operator::Le
   255	        | Operator::Gt
   256	        | Operator::Ge => eval_compare(op, &lhs, &rhs),
   257	        // Phase 0 doesn't produce Percent as a binary op (it's unary in Excel).
   258	        Operator::Percent => Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
   259	    }
   260	}

   440	    /// `StripeIndex` uses when a range's own `sheet` is `None` (today
   441	    /// every named-range conversion sets `Some(_)` so the fallback is
   442	    /// inert, but the parameter is part of the stable graph API).
   443	    fn extract_and_register_deps(
   444	        &mut self,
   445	        formula_node: NodeId,
   446	        formula_sheet: SheetId,
   447	        plan: &ExprPlan,
   448	    ) {
   449	        // First, evict any prior deps for this formula. Required so
   450	        // re-binding a formula whose text changed (e.g. `=A1` → `=B1`)
   451	        // doesn't leave the old cell-dep stamped on the session.
   452	        self.remove_formula_deps(formula_node);
   453	        // W5-50 (GAP-G-01 closure): also revoke prior graph edges +
   454	        // stripe registrations. Without this, stale forward edges
   455	        // create false `#CIRC!` cycles (megaudit H3) and stale stripe
   456	        // entries falsely dirty the formula on writes to the OLD range
   457	        // (megaudit H4). Idempotent on first-time bind.
   458	        self.graph.clear_outgoing(formula_node);
   459	        self.graph.clear_range_deps_for_formula(formula_node);
   460	
   461	        // Walk and collect.
   462	        let mut deps = FormulaDeps::default();
   463	        walk_plan_for_deps(plan, &mut deps);
   464	
   465	        // Register: deduplicate (the walker emits duplicates if the
   466	        // formula references the same cell twice; the session stores
   467	        // each cell once).
   468	        let mut seen_cells: HashSet<(SheetId, RowId, ColId)> = HashSet::new();
   469	        deps.cells.retain(|c| seen_cells.insert(*c));
   470	        let mut seen_names: HashSet<Arc<str>> = HashSet::new();
   471	        deps.names.retain(|n| seen_names.insert(Arc::clone(n)));
   472	        // named_ranges may contain the same name twice — that's
   473	        // legitimate (`SUM(Sales) + AVERAGE(Sales)` references the
   474	        // same range from two arg positions) — leave as-is.
   475	
   476	        // Phase 3.3: register direct cell deps on the session-side
   477	        // reverse index. We do NOT create CellNodes for dep cells (no
   478	        // need — the lookup at `on_set_value` is by (sheet, row, col)
   479	        // tuple, not by NodeId).
   480	        //
   481	        // Phase 3.4: ALSO add a forward `graph.add_edge(formula_node,
   482	        // dep_node)` when the dep cell is ITSELF a formula cell. The
   483	        // Tarjan scheduler walks `graph.outgoing(formula_node)` to
   484	        // discover the dep set; without this edge it can't order a
   485	        // chain like `=A1`, `=B1=A1+1`, `=C1=B1+1` correctly. The
   486	        // edge is append-only per Phase 0 contract (re-bind leaves
   487	        // stale edges — filed as GAP-G-01; impact is performance,
   488	        // not correctness, because stale edges only add constraints).
   489	        for &(s, r, c) in &deps.cells {
   490	            self.cell_to_formulas
   491	                .entry((s, r, c))
   492	                .or_default()
   493	                .insert(formula_node);
   494	            if let Some(&dep_node) = self.cell_index.get(&(s, r, c)) {
   495	                // Note: self-edges (formula_node == dep_node) are
   496	                // ALLOWED here — they're the canonical way to model
   497	                // `=A1` at A1 (self-cycle), and the Phase 0 W3-3
   498	                // Tarjan scheduler treats a self-looped single-node
   499	                // SCC as `cycled`.
   500	                self.graph.add_edge(formula_node, dep_node);
   501	            }
   502	        }
   503	        // Phase 3.3: register named-range deps with the Graph's stripe
   504	        // index. The Graph's `register_range_dependency` populates BOTH
   505	        // the stripe map AND the `formula_to_range_deps` precision-check
   506	        // map atomically. We pass the formula's owning sheet as the
   507	        // fallback (today every conversion sets `range.sheet =
   508	        // Some(_)`, so the fallback is unused).
   509	        for (_, range) in &deps.named_ranges {
   510	            let range_ref = range_to_rangeref(*range);
   511	            self.graph
   512	                .register_range_dependency(formula_node, range_ref, formula_sheet);
   513	        }
   514	
   515	        if deps.is_volatile {
   516	            self.volatile_formulas.insert(formula_node);
   517	        }
   518	        for name in &deps.names {
   519	            self.name_to_formulas
   520	                .entry(Arc::clone(name))
   521	                .or_default()
   522	                .insert(formula_node);
   523	        }
   524	        if !deps.is_empty() || deps.is_volatile {
   525	            self.formula_deps.insert(formula_node, deps);
   526	        }
   527	    }
   528	
   529	    /// Internal: remove all session-side dep state for `formula_node`.
   530	    /// Used when a formula is re-bound (different text → different
   531	    /// deps) or cleared. Cleans `formula_deps`, the volatile set, the
   532	    /// name→formulas reverse index, and the cell→formulas reverse
   533	    /// index. Does NOT touch the `Graph`'s stripe state or edges —
   534	    /// those are revoked separately by the caller via
   535	    /// `Graph::clear_outgoing` + `Graph::clear_range_deps_for_formula`
   536	    /// (W5-50). **Both callers** (`extract_and_register_deps` for
   537	    /// the rebind path, `on_clear_formula` for the clear path) MUST
   538	    /// honor this contract — the clear-path miss was caught by the
   539	    /// W5-52 mega-audit (see commit history).
   540	    fn remove_formula_deps(&mut self, formula_node: NodeId) {
   541	        if let Some(prior) = self.formula_deps.remove(&formula_node) {
   542	            for name in &prior.names {
   543	                if let Some(set) = self.name_to_formulas.get_mut(name) {
   544	                    set.remove(&formula_node);
   545	                    if set.is_empty() {
   546	                        self.name_to_formulas.remove(name);
   547	                    }
   548	                }
   549	            }
   550	            for cell in &prior.cells {
   551	                if let Some(set) = self.cell_to_formulas.get_mut(cell) {
   552	                    set.remove(&formula_node);
   553	                    if set.is_empty() {
   554	                        self.cell_to_formulas.remove(cell);
   555	                    }
   556	                }
   557	            }
   558	        }
   559	        self.volatile_formulas.remove(&formula_node);
   560	    }
   561	
   562	    /// **G3-01 acceptance.** Deterministic rebuild from an existing
   563	    /// `Workbook`. Walks every formula cell in sorted `(sheet, row, col)`
   564	    /// order, adds a `CellNode` per cell, populates the cell index,
   565	    /// and (Phase 3.2) lex+parse+binds each formula to extract
   566	    /// dependencies.
   567	    ///
   568	    /// Determinism: same workbook → same node IDs in the same order.
   569	    /// Workbook's underlying `formula_cells` is a `HashMap` (arbitrary
   570	    /// iteration order), so we sort before adding nodes.
   571	    ///
   572	    /// Failures aggregate into [`RebuildResult::failures`] rather than
   573	    /// short-circuiting — the returned session is usable, just with
   574	    /// some formulas missing dep info. Matches Phase 2B.2's
   575	    /// `RecomputeResult` shape.
   576	    pub fn rebuild_from_workbook(wb: &Workbook) -> RebuildResult {
   577	        let mut session = Self::new();
   578	
   579	        // Snapshot + sort the formula list for determinism.
   580	        let mut formulas: Vec<(SheetId, RowId, ColId, Arc<str>)> = wb
   581	            .iter_formulas()
   582	            .map(|(s, r, c, f)| (s, r, c, Arc::clone(f)))
   583	            .collect();
   584	        formulas.sort_by_key(|a| (a.0, a.1, a.2));
   585	
   586	        let attempted = formulas.len();
   587	        let mut succeeded = 0;
   588	        let mut failures: Vec<RebuildFailure> = Vec::new();
   589	
   590	        // Phase 3.4: pre-pass — insert a CellNode for every formula
   591	        // before any extract runs. The Tarjan-side forward edges
   592	        // require `cell_index` to be COMPLETE at extract time so
   593	        // formula `=A1` at C1 can see A1's node and add the edge
   594	        // (otherwise late-arriving deps would be missed). Done in
   595	        // sorted order so node IDs stay deterministic.
   596	        for (sheet, row, col, _) in &formulas {
   597	            session.or_insert_cell_node(*sheet, *row, *col);
   598	        }
   599	
   600	        for (sheet, row, col, text) in formulas {
   601	            let node = session
   602	                .cell_node_for(sheet, row, col)
   603	                .expect("pre-pass inserted every formula's node");
   604	
   605	            // Phase 3.2: lex + parse + bind + extract deps. Failure
   606	            // accumulates; we keep going. Phase 3.3 passes the
   607	            // formula's owning sheet so the stripe register can use
   608	            // the correct fallback for any range with `sheet: None`.
   609	            match Self::bind_text(&text, sheet, wb) {
   610	                Ok(plan) => {
   611	                    session.extract_and_register_deps(node, sheet, &plan);
   612	                    succeeded += 1;
   613	                }
   614	                Err(error) => failures.push(RebuildFailure {
   615	                    sheet,
   616	                    row,
   617	                    col,
   618	                    formula_text: text,
   619	                    error,
   620	                }),
   621	            }
   622	        }
   623	        // Phase 3.3: a fresh rebuild starts with NO dirty formulas —
   624	        // the caller has presumably already evaluated every cell into
   625	        // the workbook (e.g. `load_workbook_and_recompute`), so the
   626	        // session is in a clean state.
   627	        session.dirty.clear();
   628	        // Phase 3.6: aggregate cache also starts empty. Even if the
   629	        // caller pre-populated the workbook with computed values, those
   630	        // values aren't keyed by `(range, function)` and don't unlock
   631	        // a cache hit on next eval. `clear_all` is a no-op on a fresh
   632	        // session (the Default impl initializes empty), but keeping
   633	        // it makes the contract explicit.
   634	        session.aggregate_cache.clear_all();
   635	
   636	        RebuildResult {
   637	            session,
   638	            attempted,
   639	            succeeded,
   640	            failures,
   641	        }
   642	    }
   643	
   644	    /// Internal: lex + parse + bind a formula text against the
   645	    /// workbook's NameTable. Used by `rebuild_from_workbook` and by
   646	    /// `on_set_formula`'s text-only fallback. Phase 3.3 may add a
   647	    /// cached version that integrates with `PlanCache`.
   648	    fn bind_text(
   649	        text: &str,
   650	        owning_sheet: SheetId,
   651	        wb: &Workbook,
   652	    ) -> Result<ExprPlan, RuntimeError> {
   653	        let tokens = lex(text)?;
   654	        let expr = parse(tokens)?;
   655	        Ok(bind_with_names(&expr, owning_sheet, wb.names())?)
   656	    }
   657	
   658	    /// **G3-02 acceptance (hook 1/5).** Mutation hook fired by
   659	    /// `WorkbookRuntime::set_value`. Phase 3.3 (2026-05-12): the hook
   660	    /// now marks every formula that depends on `(sheet, row, col)`
   661	    /// dirty via two parallel paths:
   662	    ///
   663	    /// 1. Range deps via `Graph::dependents_for_cell` — stripe lookup
   664	    ///    + precision check (DIR-3-02 acceptance).
   665	    /// 2. Direct cell deps via the session-side `cell_to_formulas`
   666	    ///    reverse index (formulas that hold an `ExprPlan::CellRef` to
   667	    ///    this address).
   668	    ///
   669	    /// The hook DOES NOT create a node for the written cell: nodes
   670	    /// exist for FORMULA cells only; dep lookups for plain literal
   671	    /// cells go through the (sheet, row, col) tuple in both indices.
   672	    pub fn on_set_value(&mut self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId) {
   673	        self.hook_counts.set_value = self.hook_counts.set_value.saturating_add(1);
   674	        self.mark_dirty_from_cell_write(sheet, row, col);
   675	    }
   676	
   677	    /// **G3-02 acceptance (hook 2/5).** Mutation hook fired by
   678	    /// `WorkbookRuntime::set_formula`. Ensures the cell has a node,
   679	    /// walks the bound plan to register cell + range + name + volatile
   680	    /// dependencies (Phase 3.2), AND propagates dirty to downstream
   681	    /// formulas (Phase 3.3) — set_formula at a cell that other
   682	    /// formulas already reference means those downstream formulas now
   683	    /// see a different value and must recompute.
   684	    ///
   685	    /// The runtime passes the already-bound `ExprPlan` it just
   686	    /// evaluated; this hook does NOT re-bind. The plan shape is stable
   687	    /// since 2B.4 (Phase 3.2 doesn't add new variants).
   688	    pub fn on_set_formula(&mut self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId, plan: &ExprPlan) {
   689	        self.hook_counts.set_formula = self.hook_counts.set_formula.saturating_add(1);
   690	        // Phase 3.4: distinguish first-time-formula at this cell vs
   691	        // re-bind. The first case needs RETROACTIVE forward edges
   692	        // from previously-existing formulas that already reference
   693	        // this cell (those formulas' extract didn't see a NodeId for
   694	        // this cell at the time, so no edge was added).
   695	        let is_new_formula = self.cell_node_for(sheet, row, col).is_none();
   696	        let node = self.or_insert_cell_node(sheet, row, col);
   697	        if is_new_formula {
   698	            let referencing: Vec<NodeId> = self
   699	                .cell_to_formulas
   700	                .get(&(sheet, row, col))
   701	                .map(|s| s.iter().copied().collect())
   702	                .unwrap_or_default();
   703	            for prev in referencing {
   704	                if prev != node {
   705	                    self.graph.add_edge(prev, node);
   706	                }
   707	            }
   708	        }
   709	        self.extract_and_register_deps(node, sheet, plan);
   710	        // Phase 3.3: dirty downstream — formulas referencing the cell
   711	        // whose formula text just changed see a (potentially) new
   712	        // value. The formula itself is NOT marked dirty (the runtime
   713	        // wrote a fresh value for it before invoking this hook).
   714	        self.mark_dirty_from_cell_write(sheet, row, col);
   715	    }
   716	
   717	    /// **G3-02 acceptance (hook 3/5).** Mutation hook fired by
   718	    /// `WorkbookRuntime::clear_formula`. Phase 3.2: drops the cell's
   719	    /// session-side dep state (formula_deps, volatile, name reverse
   720	    /// index, cell reverse index). Phase 3.3: also marks downstream
   721	    /// formulas dirty (the cleared formula no longer produces its
   722	    /// prior value; dependents must recompute) and removes the
   723	    /// cleared formula itself from the dirty set.
   724	    ///
   725	    /// **W5-52 (audit closure):** also revoke graph-side state —
   726	    /// `clear_outgoing` (direct cell edges) + `clear_range_deps_for_formula`
   727	    /// (range deps in stripes + `formula_to_range_deps`). W5-50 wired
   728	    /// this into `extract_and_register_deps` (the rebind path) but
   729	    /// missed the clear path; Codex + Sonnet mega-audit independently
   730	    /// caught it. Without these calls, clearing a formula leaves the
   731	    /// same stale graph state that W5-50 fixes — reproducing the
   732	    /// H3/H4 staleness class on the clear path.
   733	    pub fn on_clear_formula(&mut self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId) {
   734	        self.hook_counts.clear_formula = self.hook_counts.clear_formula.saturating_add(1);
   735	        if let Some(node) = self.cell_index.get(&(sheet, row, col)).copied() {
   736	            self.remove_formula_deps(node);
   737	            // W5-52: revoke graph-side state for the cleared formula.
   738	            // Without these, stale outgoing edges + stripe entries
   739	            // persist and the next schedule_dirty can rediscover the
   740	            // cleared formula as a dependent of writes to its OLD
   741	            // deps (or as a Tarjan cycle participant if it pointed
   742	            // at a cell that later points back).
   743	            self.graph.clear_outgoing(node);
   744	            self.graph.clear_range_deps_for_formula(node);
   745	            // The cleared formula doesn't compute anymore. If it was
   746	            // dirty (queued for recompute), drop it from the set.
   747	            self.dirty.remove(&node);
   748	        }
   749	        // Downstream formulas see this cell change — propagate dirty.
   750	        self.mark_dirty_from_cell_write(sheet, row, col);
   751	    }
   752	
   753	    /// **G3-02 acceptance (hook 4/5).** Mutation hook fired by
   754	    /// `WorkbookRuntime::set_name`. Phase 3.3 (2026-05-12): marks all
   755	    /// formulas referencing `name` (per `name_to_formulas`) dirty.

   758	    /// NameRef precision gap (PlanCache `name_gen` still covers
   759	    /// correctness; per-name precision is the open follow-up).
   760	    ///

   161	    /// Add a directed edge `from -> to` ("from depends on to"). Both ids must already exist.
   162	    /// Caller is responsible for not double-adding (Phase 0 has no dedup; see edges.rs).
   163	    pub fn add_edge(&mut self, from: NodeId, to: NodeId) {
   164	        self.edges.add_edge(from, to);
   165	        self.revision = self
   166	            .revision
   167	            .checked_add(1)
   168	            .expect("Graph::add_edge: revision counter overflowed u64");
   169	    }
   170	
   171	    /// Register that `formula_node` depends on cells in `range`. Atomically updates BOTH
   172	    /// the stripe index AND `formula_to_range_deps`. Callers should invoke this once per
   173	    /// range reference in a formula's AST (a formula like `=SUM(A:A) + COUNT(B:B)` calls
   174	    /// register_range_dependency twice).
   175	    ///
   176	    /// The Phase 0 W3-5 callers (Week 4 `ql-exec` binder) own:
   177	    /// 1. Calling `add_cell_node` / `add_formula_region_node` for the formula's owning vertex.
   178	    /// 2. Calling `register_range_dependency` for each range the formula references.
   179	    /// 3. Optionally adding explicit edges via `add_edge` for direct cell-to-cell deps.
   180	    pub fn register_range_dependency(
   181	        &mut self,
   182	        formula_node: NodeId,
   183	        range: RangeRef,
   184	        sheet_id: SheetId,
   185	    ) {
   186	        assert!(
   187	            formula_node.index() < self.nodes.len(),
   188	            "Graph::register_range_dependency: formula_node {} out of bounds",
   189	            formula_node.index()
   190	        );
   191	        let inserts_before = self.stripes.total_insertions();
   192	        self.stripes.register(formula_node, &range, sheet_id);
   193	        let inserts_after = self.stripes.total_insertions();
   194	        // `stripe_inserts` counts the NEW insertions this call produced. HashSet dedup
   195	        // means a re-registration of the same (formula_node, range) might be a no-op,
   196	        // in which case the delta is zero — matches Formualizer's instrumentation
   197	        // semantics (`engine/graph/range_deps.rs:78-86`).
   198	        self.stats.stripe_inserts = self
   199	            .stats
   200	            .stripe_inserts
   201	            .checked_add((inserts_after - inserts_before) as u64)
   202	            .expect("Graph::register_range_dependency: stripe_inserts overflowed u64");
   203	        self.formula_to_range_deps
   204	            .entry(formula_node)
   205	            .or_default()
   206	            .push(range);
   207	        self.revision = self
   208	            .revision
   209	            .checked_add(1)
   210	            .expect("Graph::register_range_dependency: revision counter overflowed u64");
   211	    }
   212	
   213	    /// Borrow the instrumentation counters. Used by tests + the W3-7 graph-profile.json
   214	    /// export (OG-06 acceptance).
   215	    pub fn stats(&self) -> GraphStats {
   216	        self.stats
   217	    }

   219	    /// **W5-50 — GAP-G-01 closure.** Revoke every outgoing edge from
   220	    /// `node`, AND the symmetric back-pointer in each former target's
   221	    /// `incoming` slot. Used by `CalcgraphSession::extract_and_register_deps`
   222	    /// on re-bind so a formula whose direct cell deps changed
   223	    /// (e.g. `=A1` → `=1`) doesn't leave stale forward edges that would
   224	    /// confuse the Tarjan scheduler into seeing a false `#CIRC!` cycle.
   225	    ///
   226	    /// Bumps `revision` exactly once per call (not per edge), matching
   227	    /// the existing semantics of `add_edge` / `register_range_dependency`.
   228	    /// A node with no outgoing edges is a no-op.
   229	    ///
   230	    /// This is the documented exception to Phase 0's append-only invariant.
   231	    /// See `docs/architecture/2026-05-13-graph-storage-decision.md`.
   232	    pub fn clear_outgoing(&mut self, node: NodeId) {
   233	        assert!(
   234	            node.index() < self.nodes.len(),
   235	            "Graph::clear_outgoing: node {} out of bounds",
   236	            node.index()
   237	        );
   238	        let drained = self.edges.clear_outgoing(node);
   239	        for target in drained {
   240	            self.edges.remove_back_pointer(target, node);
   241	        }
   242	        self.revision = self
   243	            .revision
   244	            .checked_add(1)
   245	            .expect("Graph::clear_outgoing: revision counter overflowed u64");
   246	    }
   247	
   248	    /// **W5-50 — GAP-G-01 closure (range side).** Revoke every range-dep
   249	    /// registration held by `formula_node`. Clears both
   250	    /// `formula_to_range_deps[formula_node]` and every stripe membership
   251	    /// in `StripeIndex` (via `StripeIndex::clear_for_formula`). After
   252	    /// this call, writes to cells in the OLD ranges no longer dirty
   253	    /// `formula_node` via the precision-check path.
   254	    ///
   255	    /// Bumps `revision` once per call. Idempotent — a formula with no
   256	    /// range deps is a no-op.
   257	    ///
   258	    /// Companion to `clear_outgoing`: the session-side `extract_and_register_deps`
   259	    /// calls both before re-extracting the formula's new dep set.
   260	    pub fn clear_range_deps_for_formula(&mut self, formula_node: NodeId) {

     1	//! Stripe index — `(sheet, axis, index) → set of dependent NodeIds`. The A5 acceptance
     2	//! piece: given a cell-coordinate write, find all formulas that reference a range
     3	//! containing that cell, in time proportional to the number of distinct stripes touched
     4	//! (not the number of formulas).
     5	//!
     6	//! ## Pattern (CORR-21)
     7	//!
     8	//! Per the Week 3 Day 0 reference deep-read (`docs/phase0/references-reading-log.md`),
     9	//! Formualizer's `engine/graph/range_deps.rs:36-192` is the closest precedent. The
    10	//! pattern:
    11	//!
    12	//! - `StripeKey { sheet_id, stripe_type: Row | Column, index: u32 }`.
    13	//! - `stripe_to_dependents: HashMap<StripeKey, HashSet<NodeId>>`.
    14	//! - At range registration, a **shape heuristic** picks the cheaper axis to index along:
    15	//!   `if height > width → Column stripes`, `else → Row stripes`. So `SUM(A:A)` registers
    16	//!   ONE Column-stripe entry (col 0); `SUM(A1:A1000)` also ONE Column entry; `SUM(A1:Z1)`
    17	//!   ONE Row entry. A range `A1:Z1000` with height==width=tie goes to Row stripes (the
    18	//!   `else` branch).
    19	//!
    20	//! We intentionally **skip** Formualizer's optional 256×256 Block stripes for Phase 0.
    21	//! They add a third stripe axis only when `enable_block_stripes` is set AND the range
    22	//! is 2D; the A5 falsifier doesn't need them. Revisit in Week 4 if A1 acceptance
    23	//! reveals 2D-region perf cliffs.
    24	//!
    25	//! ## Precision-check requirement
    26	//!
    27	//! The stripe map is **coarser** than the range. `SUM(A1:A10)` registers under Column 0,
    28	//! but a write to `A500` would falsely match. Callers MUST cross-check each candidate
    29	//! returned by `dependents_for_cell` against the formula's actual `RangeRef` (stored on
    30	//! `Graph::formula_to_range_deps`) before considering it a true dependent.
    31	//!
    32	//! `Graph::dependents_for_cell` (added in this commit) wraps both steps.
    33	
    34	use std::collections::{HashMap, HashSet};
    35	
    36	use ql_formula_syntax::RangeRef;
    37	use ql_types::{ColId, RowId, SheetId};
    38	
    39	use crate::node::NodeId;
    40	
    41	/// Axis discriminator for `StripeKey`. Phase 0 has only Row and Column; Block is
    42	/// deferred (see module doc).
    43	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, PartialOrd, Ord)]
    44	pub enum StripeType {
    45	    Row,
    46	    Column,
    47	}
    48	
    49	/// A single stripe identifier: `(sheet, axis, index)`. For `StripeType::Row`, `index` is
    50	/// a `RowId`; for `StripeType::Column`, a `ColId`. Stored as `u32` since both type-alias
    51	/// to `u32` upstream.
    52	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, PartialOrd, Ord)]
    53	pub struct StripeKey {
    54	    pub sheet_id: SheetId,
    55	    pub stripe_type: StripeType,
    56	    pub index: u32,
    57	}
    58	
    59	/// Sparse per-stripe index of which formulas depend on each stripe.
    60	///
    61	/// Use via `Graph::register_range_dependency` and `Graph::dependents_for_cell` — those
    62	/// wrap the stripe insertion and precision-check together.
    63	///
    64	/// ## Reverse index (Phase 4 / W5-50)
    65	///
    66	/// `formula_to_stripe_keys: HashMap<NodeId, Vec<StripeKey>>` records, per formula
    67	/// node, every stripe key it has been registered against. Used by
    68	/// `clear_for_formula` to revoke a formula's stripe membership on re-bind in
    69	/// O(stripes-this-formula-is-in) instead of scanning the whole stripe map. The
    70	/// reverse vector is append-on-newly-inserted (matches the forward
    71	/// `HashSet::insert` returning `true`), so a re-registration of the same
    72	/// `(formula, range)` does not duplicate the reverse entry.
    73	#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default)]
    74	pub struct StripeIndex {
    75	    stripe_to_dependents: HashMap<StripeKey, HashSet<NodeId>>,
    76	    /// Reverse lookup: which stripes does `formula_node` appear in? Mirror of
    77	    /// `stripe_to_dependents` keyed by formula. Kept in lock-step with the
    78	    /// forward map via the `insert` helper.
    79	    formula_to_stripe_keys: HashMap<NodeId, Vec<StripeKey>>,
    80	}
    81	
    82	impl StripeIndex {
    83	    pub fn new() -> Self {
    84	        Self::default()
    85	    }
    86	
    87	    /// Distinct stripe keys present in the map. Used by tests + W3-7 graph-profile export.
    88	    pub fn stripe_count(&self) -> usize {
    89	        self.stripe_to_dependents.len()
    90	    }
    91	
    92	    /// Total `(stripe_key, formula_node)` insertions across all stripes. The A5 acceptance
    93	    /// test asserts this scales linearly with formula count (1 insert per formula via the
    94	    /// shape heuristic), NOT with range size.
    95	    pub fn total_insertions(&self) -> usize {
    96	        self.stripe_to_dependents.values().map(|s| s.len()).sum()
    97	    }
    98	
    99	    /// Test-only read access for A4 structural assertions (CORR-24 typed accessor pattern).
   100	    #[cfg(test)]
   101	    pub(crate) fn raw_map(&self) -> &HashMap<StripeKey, HashSet<NodeId>> {
   102	        &self.stripe_to_dependents
   103	    }
   104	
   105	    /// Register `formula_node`'s dependency on `range`. Picks the cheaper-to-index axis
   106	    /// via the shape heuristic; inserts one entry per touched stripe index along that
   107	    /// axis. Resolves the `RangeRef`'s optional sheet to `sheet_id` if `None`.
   108	    ///
   109	    /// **Panics** if the `range` has reversed bounds (start > end on any axis). Per the
   110	    /// no-fallbacks rule (audit finding H1, 2026-05-12): malformed ranges previously
   111	    /// silently no-op'd the stripe insert because `for c in start..=end` is an empty
   112	    /// range when `start > end`. The formula would have appeared registered (entry in
   113	    /// `formula_to_range_deps`) but be unreachable via writes — silent dependency loss.
   114	    /// Now: loud panic at the boundary. Caller is responsible for normalizing before
   115	    /// calling.
   116	    pub fn register(&mut self, formula_node: NodeId, range: &RangeRef, sheet_id: SheetId) {
   117	        match range {
   118	            RangeRef::Cells {
   119	                sheet,
   120	                start_col,
   121	                start_row,
   122	                end_col,
   123	                end_row,
   124	                ..
   125	            } => {
   126	                assert!(
   127	                    start_col <= end_col,
   128	                    "StripeIndex::register: Cells range has reversed cols (start_col={start_col} > end_col={end_col})"
   129	                );
   130	                assert!(
   131	                    start_row <= end_row,
   132	                    "StripeIndex::register: Cells range has reversed rows (start_row={start_row} > end_row={end_row})"
   133	                );
   134	                let s = sheet.unwrap_or(sheet_id);
   135	                let height = end_row - start_row + 1;
   136	                let width = end_col - start_col + 1;
   137	                if height > width {
   138	                    for c in *start_col..=*end_col {
   139	                        self.insert(
   140	                            StripeKey {
   141	                                sheet_id: s,
   142	                                stripe_type: StripeType::Column,
   143	                                index: c,
   144	                            },
   145	                            formula_node,
   146	                        );
   147	                    }
   148	                } else {
   149	                    for r in *start_row..=*end_row {
   150	                        self.insert(
   151	                            StripeKey {
   152	                                sheet_id: s,
   153	                                stripe_type: StripeType::Row,
   154	                                index: r,
   155	                            },
   156	                            formula_node,
   157	                        );
   158	                    }
   159	                }
   160	            }
   161	            RangeRef::WholeColumn {
   162	                sheet,
   163	                start_col,
   164	                end_col,
   165	                ..
   166	            } => {
   167	                assert!(
   168	                    start_col <= end_col,
   169	                    "StripeIndex::register: WholeColumn has reversed cols (start_col={start_col} > end_col={end_col})"
   170	                );
   171	                let s = sheet.unwrap_or(sheet_id);
   172	                for c in *start_col..=*end_col {
   173	                    self.insert(
   174	                        StripeKey {
   175	                            sheet_id: s,
   176	                            stripe_type: StripeType::Column,
   177	                            index: c,
   178	                        },
   179	                        formula_node,
   180	                    );
   181	                }
   182	            }
   183	            RangeRef::WholeRow {
   184	                sheet,
   185	                start_row,
   186	                end_row,
   187	                ..
   188	            } => {
   189	                assert!(
   190	                    start_row <= end_row,
   191	                    "StripeIndex::register: WholeRow has reversed rows (start_row={start_row} > end_row={end_row})"
   192	                );
   193	                let s = sheet.unwrap_or(sheet_id);
   194	                for r in *start_row..=*end_row {
   195	                    self.insert(
   196	                        StripeKey {
   197	                            sheet_id: s,
   198	                            stripe_type: StripeType::Row,
   199	                            index: r,
   200	                        },
   201	                        formula_node,
   202	                    );
   203	                }
   204	            }
   205	        }
   206	    }
   207	
   208	    fn insert(&mut self, key: StripeKey, formula_node: NodeId) {
   209	        let newly_inserted = self
   210	            .stripe_to_dependents
   211	            .entry(key)
   212	            .or_default()
   213	            .insert(formula_node);
   214	        // Mirror into the reverse index ONLY when the forward HashSet
   215	        // accepted the entry as new. Re-registering the same
   216	        // `(formula, range)` is a no-op on both sides — the original A5
   217	        // stripe-inserts counter semantics rely on this.
   218	        if newly_inserted {
   219	            self.formula_to_stripe_keys
   220	                .entry(formula_node)
   221	                .or_default()
   222	                .push(key);
   223	        }
   224	    }
   225	
   226	    /// Revoke every stripe membership held by `formula_node`. Called by
   227	    /// `Graph::clear_range_deps_for_formula` on re-bind so a formula whose
   228	    /// range deps changed (`SUM(A:A) → SUM(B:B)`) no longer falsely dirty
   229	    /// from writes to the OLD range.
   230	    ///
   231	    /// O(stripes-this-formula-is-in) thanks to the reverse index. A
   232	    /// formula with no stripe membership (e.g., one that never called
   233	    /// `register_range_dependency`) is a no-op.
   234	    ///
   235	    /// Empties stripe buckets are pruned: if `formula_node` was the last
   236	    /// dependent on a stripe, the `StripeKey` entry is removed entirely.
   237	    /// This keeps `stripe_count()` honest.
   238	    ///
   239	    /// Idempotent: calling twice on the same node yields the same final
   240	    /// state.
   241	    pub fn clear_for_formula(&mut self, formula_node: NodeId) {
   242	        let Some(keys) = self.formula_to_stripe_keys.remove(&formula_node) else {
   243	            return;
   244	        };
   245	        for key in keys {
   246	            if let Some(set) = self.stripe_to_dependents.get_mut(&key) {
   247	                set.remove(&formula_node);
   248	                if set.is_empty() {
   249	                    self.stripe_to_dependents.remove(&key);
   250	                }
   251	            }
   252	        }
   253	    }
   254	
   255	    /// Test-only typed accessor for the reverse index (CORR-24 pattern).
   256	    /// Returns the list of stripe keys the formula appears under, or
   257	    /// `None` if it's never been registered.
   258	    #[cfg(test)]
   259	    pub(crate) fn stripe_keys_for_formula(&self, formula_node: NodeId) -> Option<&Vec<StripeKey>> {
   260	        self.formula_to_stripe_keys.get(&formula_node)

exec

Biggest pre-handoff fixes are doc drift: `docs/MASTER-PLAN.md` still has stale header metadata at lines 3-5, `docs/known-gaps.md` still says GAP-F-01 is “22 of ~260” at line 69 despite 102 registry entries, and `docs/architecture/2026-05-13-graph-storage-decision.md` still opens with “NOT shipped” at line 3 even though W5-50/W5-52 shipped and amended it later. I could not save the verdict file because this Codex session is read-only; the existing `session-finalization-planning-codex.txt` is the prompt transcript, not my verdict.

   Enforceable process doc. This is missing today; `docs/process/` does not exist. This should be created and treated as mandatory reading for every next phase.

   Final audit of W5-53..W5-58, or a scoped “session final audit queue” if the actual audit is deferred to a separate dispatch. The current W5-52 audit only covers W5-49..W5-51.

## 2. Audit-protocol design

`docs/process/audit-protocol.md` should be prescriptive:

- **Mandatory at session start:** read latest handoff, `MASTER-PLAN.md`, `known-gaps.md`, compat matrix, relevant architecture docs, and current code hotspots.
- **Mandatory before architecture work:** use Plan mode, dispatch Codex before implementation, record decision in `docs/architecture/<date>-<topic>.md`, then implement against that decision.
- **Mandatory after every shippable commit:** run 7 gates: `cargo fmt --all --check`, clippy with `-D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace`, build-flags guard, cargo-lock pin guard, multiversion clone/disasm guard, `cargo audit`.
- **Mandatory after every phase or substantial run:** independent audit pass using the W5-52 pattern: Codex + at least one second auditor for high-risk graph/runtime/function semantics. Save prompts and outputs under `docs/audits/`.
- **Mandatory audit closure:** HIGH findings fixed before moving on; MEDIUM either fixed or filed in `known-gaps.md` with target phase; LOW/doc drift either fixed immediately or explicitly deferred.
- **Mandatory cadence:** lightweight self-audit after each shippable commit; external/parallel audit after each phase, architectural change, or 1-2 implementation cycles. For risky substrate changes, audit every run, not only every phase.
- **Mandatory cycle limit:** ≤2 plan-implement-audit cycles per session. If a third cycle is needed, write a handoff and stop.
- **Mandatory environment command form:** use the OrbStack bridge:
  `mac zsh -lc 'export PATH="$HOME/.cargo/bin:$PATH"; cd /Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine && cargo test --locked --workspace'`
- **Recommended:** targeted tests before full gates, compat reporter after matrix edits, and `git diff --check` before commit.
- **Forbidden:** claiming “phase complete” without docs/gaps/matrix updates.

## 3. Pre-handoff fixes

Fix these before handing off:

- `docs/MASTER-PLAN.md:3-5` still says date `2026-05-12` and HEAD `fc977815cd2`. Update to current date and `d6a6bcdcfc5`.
- `docs/known-gaps.md:69` still says GAP-F-01 is “22 of ~260”. It should say 102 registry entries, wave-1 100 target closed, full ~260 target remains Phase 4.10.
- `docs/architecture/2026-05-13-graph-storage-decision.md:3` says “Implementation queued… NOT shipped.” Later lines 177-187 amend this, but the header is misleading. Change status to shipped W5-50, audit-closed W5-52, GAP-F-05 closed W5-53.
- `docs/compat/excel-matrix.md:97` says logical `IFS` is ❌. That is probably correct, but W5-55 commit wording “IFS family” is ambiguous. Handoff should say “conditional aggregate IFS family: SUMIFS/COUNTIFS/AVERAGEIFS, not logical IFS.”
- `crates/ql-exec/src/calcgraph_session.rs:486-488` still comments that append-only stale edges are performance not correctness. That is stale after W5-50; update because the code now calls `clear_outgoing` at lines 453-459.
- `crates/ql-exec/src/scalar.rs:72-78` still says `AggregateNameRef` evaluates to `#CALC!` pending Phase 3.6 in the legacy `eval_scalar` path. This may be true for that legacy entry point, but the comment can mislead because `eval_scalar_with_cache` implements aggregate eval. Clarify “legacy no-registry path only.”
- `crates/ql-exec/src/plan.rs:159-183` says the hardcoded aggregate list will be replaced in Phase 4.3. Phase 4.3 wave 1 is now closed and the whitelist still exists. Retarget replacement to Phase 4.7/4.10 or “before arrays/per-function metadata.”
- `docs/compat/excel-matrix.md:116-117` already documents CEILING/FLOOR zero-significance divergence. Make it prominent in handoff gotchas because it is easy to “normalize” incorrectly.
- Named-range cell-ref collision from W5-55 should be documented in the final handoff and audit protocol. I did not see it in current docs.

## 4. Gotchas

Your list is right. Add these:

- Existing root `session-finalization-planning-codex.txt` is not the verdict; it is the Codex prompt transcript. Save final outputs under `docs/audits/` with clear names.
- `docs/process/` does not exist; creation is part of the finalization work.
- The repo root is VS Code-sized; broad `rg` can return huge unrelated files. Scope searches to `quantbook-engine/` unless intentionally checking workspace hooks.
- Git emits macOS `/tmp/xcrun_db` warnings under read-only/sandbox contexts. Do not treat those as repo failures.
- `FunctionRegistry::names()` at `registry.rs:115-117` only returns scalar names, not range-aware names. Any future registry metadata or tests must account for both tables.
- Range-aware dispatch checks `lookup_range_aware` first at `scalar.rs:143-163`; registering a range-aware function as scalar will silently lose range shape.
- `FnArg::Range` shape is row-major and consumed by lookup/index functions. Shape bugs will pass SUMIF/COUNTIF tests.
- `is_aggregate_function` is really “allows named/range args in binder,” not pure aggregate semantics. The name is now misleading but pinned by tests.
- `Graph::clear_outgoing` and `clear_range_deps_for_formula` bump revision even on no-op at `graph.rs:232-246` and `255-260`; documented low risk.
- Cross-sheet refs are Phase 4.6; current range supplemental logic has future hooks but must be re-audited when sheet refs land.

## 5. Optimal next phase

Do not jump straight to dates or cross-sheet refs. Recommended next five sessions:

1. **Finalization + W5-53..W5-58 audit closure.**
   Fix doc drift above, write audit protocol, produce final handoff, dispatch actual audit for the 10 commits.

2. **Phase 4.3 polish micro-batch.**
   Wildcards in SUMIF/COUNTIF/SEARCH, PROPER, CLEAN, CONCAT range-aware, RANK.AVG, CEILING.MATH/FLOOR.MATH. Keep MODE.MULT deferred because spill support is Phase 4.7.

3. **Phase 4.4 coercion/error semantics design.**
   Use W5-49-style Codex-reviewed architecture doc. This is foundational and should happen before dates, arrays, xlsx, and broader function wave 2.

4. **Phase 4.5 dates/times/number formats.**
   Substantial but unavoidable. Excel epoch policy and format parser need a real design packet.

5. **Phase 4.6 cross-sheet refs + sheet-scoped names.**
   Natural after coercion/date foundations. Re-audit graph supplemental and named-range behavior here.

Then Phase 4.7 arrays/spills/FormulaRegion binder, Phase 4.8 tables, Phase 4.9 R1C1/localization/implicit intersection, Phase 4.10 wave 2, Phase 4.11 xlsx, Phase 4.12 megaudit.

## 6. First-10-min script

1. `cd /Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine`
2. `git status --short && git log --oneline -20`
3. Read:
   `docs/audits/2026-05-13-engine-session-final-handoff.md`
   `docs/process/audit-protocol.md`
   `docs/MASTER-PLAN.md`
   `docs/known-gaps.md`
   `docs/compat/excel-matrix.md`
4. Verify state before acting:
   `mac zsh -lc 'export PATH="$HOME/.cargo/bin:$PATH"; cd /Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine && cargo test --locked --workspace'`
5. Check registry count and whitelist:
   inspect `crates/ql-functions/src/registry.rs` and `crates/ql-exec/src/plan.rs`.
6. Decide first work mode:
   if final audit not done, do audit first; if audit clean, start Phase 4.3 polish.
7. If non-trivial design appears, stop implementation and dispatch Codex before coding.
8. After any commit candidate, run all 7 gates through the `mac zsh -lc` bridge.

## 7. Audit-skipping risks

The biggest risk is quiet doc/code divergence becoming the next window’s starting premise. That already almost happened: W5-50 missed `on_clear_formula`, and W5-52 caught it only because both Codex and Sonnet audited the same surface. Without a baked protocol, the next window can build dates, cross-sheet refs, or arrays on stale assumptions: wrong registry counts, wrong gap status, incorrect aggregate whitelist semantics, or “G-01/G-03 shipped” without remembering the VEQ/supplemental watch list.

The failure mode is not just bugs. It is false confidence: commit messages say “all green,” docs say “unblocked,” and the next engineer skips the adversarial read. The protocol forces every phase to leave an auditable trail: what changed, what was checked, what remains unsafe, and what the next window must not assume.

## 7. Audit-skipping risks

The biggest risk is quiet doc/code divergence becoming the next window’s starting premise. That already almost happened: W5-50 missed `on_clear_formula`, and W5-52 caught it only because both Codex and Sonnet audited the same surface. Without a baked protocol, the next window can build dates, cross-sheet refs, or arrays on stale assumptions: wrong registry counts, wrong gap status, incorrect aggregate whitelist semantics, or “G-01/G-03 shipped” without remembering the VEQ/supplemental watch list.

The failure mode is not just bugs. It is false confidence: commit messages say “all green,” docs say “unblocked,” and the next engineer skips the adversarial read. The protocol forces every phase to leave an auditable trail: what changed, what was checked, what remains unsafe, and what the next window must not assume.
